**Document conventions (enforced 2026-07-10):** Do not use long dashes (em or en dashes) anywhere in this file, or in any copy produced for JRS (connection notes, invitations, prospectuses, site prose). Use a colon, comma, or parentheses instead. This mirrors the house rule in CLAUDE.md and a standing instruction from Phillip.

**Standing directive (Phillip, 2026-07-15): every time this tracker is updated, deliver a fresh copy of the file to Phillip in the same turn (attach `research/MASTER_TRACKER.md`), without being asked again.** Applies whenever any edit is made to this file, not only on an explicit "provide copy" request.

**Standing directive (Phillip, 2026-07-23): update this tracker on EVERY response.** Every turn logs a dated one-line entry in the running session log (Section 15), even for pure question/answer or advice turns with no file artifact: record the decision, analysis, or asset produced. Then deliver the fresh copy per the directive above. Mirrored in CLAUDE.md Section VIII so it survives context compaction.

**Standing directive (completion verification, 2026-07-20, after the four-caveat incident):** NO completion recognition of any kind (certificate, reference letter, LinkedIn recommendation, completer thank-you, a tracker "Complete" status, or an aggregate roll-up) may be produced from a verbal or secondhand report alone. FIRST run **`python3 research/check_completion.py <CODE>`** (works for Arm A `V-AI-##` and Arm B `RR-###`; reads the anon-readable `pilot_progress` / `armb_progress` views, no service key needed; exit 0 = complete). If it reports NOT COMPLETE or NO ROW, stop and report the discrepancy to Phillip instead of building the package. Never write "per Phillip; DB pull to confirm" again: the pull takes one command, so do it before writing anything.

**Standing directive (Phillip, 2026-07-13):** always take whatever legitimate steps raise IP value and move toward a sale. Operating plan and prioritized path: `research/IP_Sale_Playbook.md`. Integrity rule governs all of it: only real, verifiable assets and claims (diligence tests every number).

**Authorship policy (enforced 2026-07-11):** Reviewers who complete the study are recognized as named contributors only (certificate, named international panel, acknowledgment in any write-up). They are NOT offered co-authorship. Co-authorship is reserved for the specific collaborators doing substantial analysis or writing (currently Stacy Young on the FOIL paper; Sanya Dalal, pending, on the Business Ethics paper; and Ubayet Hossain, offered 2026-07-14 and pending, as co-author on EACH paper that uses his methodology across HR, compliance, and AI governance, for the reliability/validation methodology he designed). Integrity guardrail: his co-authorship on any given paper is legitimate only if he reviews and signs off on that specific manuscript (the offer message commits to sending each draft for his review), which keeps it earned authorship, not courtesy. Future prospectuses omit the co-authorship line entirely: the recognition section ends at "Featured by name among the international panel of professionals in any publication or write-up." with no co-author clause.

**STANDING RULE, NO LINKS IN COMPLETION MESSAGES (owner directive, stated twice: 2026-08-04 and earlier; violated twice by the assistant, do not repeat):** completer thank-you messages, certificates, and recognition messages carry **NO contributor confirmation links, no registration links, and no new site links of any kind**. The owner is not distributing contributor links until the study is CLOSED. A completion message contains the thank-you, the attached certificate, and the promises already made (named recognition, reference, LinkedIn recommendation, results summary at closure). Nothing else. This applies to every completer message, Arm A and Arm B, now and in future. The Arm B rule is stricter still and unchanged: no JRS-branded link of any kind, ever, while that arm is blind.

**Acknowledgment is not endorsement (clarified 2026-07-13, prompted by Marguerite Maroudis, PhD):** naming a reviewer reflects participation in the validation study only. Reviewers are credited as independent reviewers or contributors, never as endorsers or validators of JRS. Attribution is opt-in, reviewers approve the exact public wording before it appears, and may withdraw before publication. This distinction is now stated in the prospectus consent section.

**Open co-authorship item:** SungSoo In (V-AI-24) received the earlier conditional offer and has been given a genuine "maybe" (2026-07-11): revisit once the initial detection results are in. He has consented to named panel recognition. If authorship is ultimately not shared, convert his recognition to acknowledged contributor, not a writing seat. **UPDATE 2026-07-22:** SungSoo completed the six training modules (JRS Decision Defensibility Reviewer Program) and sent a gracious note: reiterated interest in a meaningful role/co-authorship once validation results are in (explicitly no-pressure, understands it is paper-dependent), and asked whether his own **Athena Governance Architecture** (authority boundaries, runtime governance, preserving conditions/reasoning behind governed decisions) could complement JRS, offering a concise overview. Reply drafted (`research/Message_SungSoo_In_Reply.md`), **FIRMER-BOUNDARY version (Phillip, 2026-07-22): Phillip doubts Athena is a legitimate company; a web check found NO link between "Athena Governance Architecture" and SungSoo In (only unrelated Athena governance projects), so treat it as a self-styled personal framework, NOT a verifiable company (same posture as Andrey/EAS, Jake/Institute for Higher Thinking).** Reply now: warm appreciation for his REAL, DB-verified reviewer + training contribution; role stays a genuine no-pressure maybe (recognition already secured); and the Athena door is CLOSED cleanly, JRS stays fully self-contained through validation, NO integration, NO offer to review Athena materials, NO partnership, framed as protecting validation independence, not a judgment of his work. **GUARDRAIL (Richard Whitney/MIR precedent, now firmer): do NOT couple JRS to Athena, do NOT lean on Athena for any credibility, and do NOT present it as a verified entity in any diligence/marketing.** He remains a valued reviewer (24-record review + all 6 modules); keep the person-level relationship warm, the framework-level boundary firm.

**Hekim Colpan (V-AI-20), 2026-07-13:** sent Phillip a reciprocal LinkedIn recommendation (received; Phillip should accept/publish it) and offered to stay involved and contribute to the analysis and development. Same handling as SungSoo: welcome his continued input, no co-authorship promised; revisit only if a substantial written contribution arises. His voluntary recommendation is his own endorsement (fine and valuable); distinct from our naming him, which remains participation-only.

## 1. Validation ladder: where each stage stands

| Stage | Question | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Rung 1: Reproducibility** | Do 3 independent AI models (Claude, GPT, Gemini) give the same JRS read on the same record? | ✅ 84% agreement across 15 records. Auto-runs nightly. |
| **Rung 2a: Reliability** | Do independent human reviewers agree with each other? | Preliminary; reviewer sample still being collected, not yet reported. |
| **Rung 2b: Accuracy** | Can reviewers match a hidden answer key on 24 records? | 🟡 **13 of ~26 reviewers complete** (Jake, Frank, Lawal, Andrey, Hekim, Kyle, SungSoo, Gabi Cortez, Saurabh, Nigel, Nitin, Andres V-AI-30 + **Dr Gabriela Bar V-AI-16**; DB-verified 2026-07-29 via `check_completion.py`; Niloofar V-AI-23 at 22/24). Key verified 24/24. (V-AI-17 withdrew 2026-07-16.) |
| **Construct validity** | Are the five conditions distinct dimensions? | ⚪ NOT REQUIRED for sale/primary papers (decision 2026-07-20): off the pre-registered detection plan and off the sale critical path. Data thin for a formal factor analysis (108 labels, 10 records). Optional/deferred, see decision entry. |
| **Rung 3: Criterion validity** | Do flagged records fail in real cases? | 🟡 12 real cases collected. |
| **External validity** | Does it hold on real (non-constructed) records? | ⬜ Future. |

### The pilots: evidence collected (verified live)
| # | Pilot | What it collects | Evidence to date |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **AI-Assisted Records Detection** (Study 011) | Reviewer reads on the 24-record set vs a verified key | **13 complete of ~26 registered (Jake, Frank, Lawal, Andrey, Hekim, Kyle, SungSoo, Gabi Cortez, Saurabh, Nigel, Nitin, Andres V-AI-30, Dr Gabriela Bar V-AI-16; DB-verified 2026-07-28 via `check_completion.py`; SungSoo 48, Nigel 48, Saurabh 31, Jake 25 include resubmissions; Niloofar V-AI-23 22/24 in progress)** (V-AI-17 withdrew 2026-07-16) |
| 2 | **Bench Reliability** (Studies 003/004) | Expert + reviewer scoring of a shared record set | Preliminary; reviewer sample still being collected, not yet reported |
| 3 | **Real-Case Criterion** (Study 010, Rung 3) | Real public cases + documented outcomes, in 3 domain pilots | **12 real cases: FOIL/Stacy 7, HR/Tanvi 5, Healthcare/Keith 0 (breakdown in §2)** |

**How the rungs map to the site's study registry (research.html):**
| Rung / stage | Site study |
|---|---|
| Rung 1 Reproducibility | Study 001 (AI Reproducibility) |
| Rung 2a Reliability | Studies 003/004 (condition performance / reviewer agreement) |
| Rung 2b Accuracy | Study 011 (AI-Assisted Records Detection) |
| Construct validity | Study 009 (Organizational-Psychology Readiness) |
| Rung 3 Criterion validity | Study 010 (Criterion Validity, real-outcome) |

- **Co-author:** Stacy Young (E-08), Records Governance Advisor and Public-Records Domain Lead (Deputy Records Access Officer, NYC HPD).
- **Basis:** the Public-Records Documentation Validation Pilot (PR-DVP); real public determinations paired with documented outcomes; JRS read recorded blind to outcome.

- **Target / deadline:** 20-30 cases with a spread of outcomes; first-batch and full-set deadlines per the pilot (full set by Aug 31, 2026).
- **Positioning (per the May 28, 2026 Chief FOIA Officers Council technology initiative):** JRS evaluates the reviewability of records produced by any workflow (manual or AI-assisted); it is a governance layer above any FOIA technology, complementary to technology inventories that measure software but not record reviewability.
- **Gate (no fabrication):** not submittable until n reaches the pre-registered target with a spread of outcomes; Results/Abstract remain placeholders until then.

- **Gates (no fabrication / no duplication):** (1) no criterion claim until HR and public-records pilots reach 20-30 with a spread of outcomes; (2) the public-records primary results live in the FOIL paper only: used illustratively here to avoid duplicate publication; (3) authorship assigned by substantive intellectual contribution (ethics-journal integrity).

## 9. Field Guide distribution & download counter (built 2026-07-10)

**Why:** the three Investigator Field Guides (EEO, Fair Housing, International) are meant to be shared off-site (emailed, forwarded, mentioned in a Dewey case alert), so on-site GA4 `file_download` events undercount badly. A redirect counter captures every download regardless of where the link is clicked, and tags it by channel.

**How it works:** each download link points at the edge function `/api/dl?e=<edition>&src=<channel>`, which writes one append-only row to `public.guide_downloads` and 302-redirects to the static PDF. The DB write is best-effort: if Supabase is down or the service key is unset, the user still gets the file.

| Piece | Location |
|---|---|
| Edge function | `api/dl.js` (Vercel edge; uses `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`) |
| Table + private aggregate view | `public.guide_downloads`, `public.guide_download_counts` (RLS on, no anon read) |
| Repointed links | `investigator-guides.html` (3 on-site links tagged `src=site`) |

**Edition tokens:** `employment`, `fairhousing`, `international`.

**Per-channel share links** (use the right `src` so you learn which channel works):
- Site (already wired): `/api/dl?e=employment&src=site`
- Dewey author intros: `https://www.jrsstandard.com/api/dl?e=employment&src=dewey`
- LinkedIn post/comment: `https://www.jrsstandard.com/api/dl?e=employment&src=linkedin`
- AWI: `https://www.jrsstandard.com/api/dl?e=employment&src=awi`
(swap `employment` for `fairhousing` / `international` as needed)

**Read the counts** (Supabase SQL editor or service role): `select * from public.guide_download_counts;`

**Status:** LIVE in production as of 2026-07-10. Table applied to Supabase; `api/dl.js` + repointed `investigator-guides.html` deployed to `main` (commit `57534d8`) via a **selective deploy**: only those two files were taken onto `main` from `origin/main`, so `research/` (answer key included) and the other unreviewed dev changes did NOT ship. Verified live: `GET /api/dl?e=employment` returns 302 → the EEO PDF. Two `deploytest` rows created during verification were deleted; the table starts clean.

**Deploy note (important):** production `main` currently contains **no `research/` files** and there is **no `.vercelignore`**. Do NOT deploy by pushing the whole dev branch to `main`: that would publish the answer key and the private `pilot-status.html` dashboard. Always deploy selected public files only (as was done here).

**Dashboard charts (added 2026-07-11, LIVE):** `pilot-status.html` now shows two inline charts (no external library, matching the site's no-dependency convention).
- **Panel completion:** horizontal stacked bar (Complete / In progress / Not started), computed client-side from `pilot_progress`.
- **Investigator Guide Downloads by day:** stacked bar chart colored by edition, from a new aggregate-only view `public.guide_downloads_public` (day x edition counts, granted to anon; the raw `guide_downloads` log stays private). Categorical palette validated for the dark surface: EEO `#3987e5`, Fair Housing `#199e70`, International `#c98500`.
- Deployed to `main` via selective deploy (commit `73b57f1`); only `pilot-status.html` shipped.

## 10. Publication sequencing & strategy (three papers in flight)

**Principle (from the plan review, 2026-07-10):** the *study* is the contribution, not the framework. A publication is a credibility anchor for the IP, not the finish line: pair it with at least one real-org pilot (a demand signal), which a buyer weighs more than any single paper. Post a **preprint** (OSF/SSRN) at submission so credibility exists before peer review clears (realistically 9-15 months). Scope every claim to what the data shows and honor the pre-registered floors.

**The three papers (all already in the tracker: see §6, §7, §8):**

| # | Paper | Venue | Co-author | Data basis | Gating item |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | **Detection**: "Detecting Decision Reconstruction Risk in AI-Assisted Documentation" | AI and Ethics (alt: AI & Society; FAccT/AIES workshop) | panel; Ubayet methodology credited | Study 011: 24-record set vs verified key (+ Arm B if run) | Finish the panel (7/26 complete); decide Arm B |
| B | **FOIL**: "Documentation as a Governance Layer" | Journal of Civic Information | **Stacy Young (E-08): CONFIRMED** | Public-records pilot n=7 → target 20-30 | Reach 20-30 cases with a spread (Aug 31, 2026) |
| C | **Business Ethics**: "Documentation Governance in AI-Assisted Decision-Making" | Journal of Business Ethics | **Sanya Dalal (pending)** | **Tanvi HR pilot n=5** + Rungs 1-2; public-records illustrative only | Sanya yes/no; HR data maturity; no duplication with FOIL |

**Sequence:**
1. **Submit Paper A (Detection) first.** It is the novel-contribution anchor, and its hard-to-copy asset is the international blind panel + verified answer key. It reaches "submittable" by finishing the panel (recruiting/nudging you control), not by slow real-world case accrual. **Arm B decision:** run it for the strong "JRS beats unaided review" claim, or skip it and scope Paper A to "accuracy against a verified key." Preprint at submission.
2. **Paper B (FOIL) second.** Co-author confirmed; gated only on case accrual to 20-30 (deadline Aug 31, 2026). Submit once n≥20 with a spread of outcomes.
3. **Paper C (Business Ethics) third.** Gated on Sanya's acceptance AND the HR/criterion data maturing; must not duplicate FOIL's primary results. Sanya's yes also unblocks telling Tanvi about the paper.

**Immediate actions (parallel, now):**
- Nudge the 19 unfinished detection reviewers (binding task for Paper A).
- Decide Arm B (run vs scope-down).
- Get Sanya's yes/no (unblocks Paper C + Tanvi).
- Keep FOIL and HR case collection moving toward 20-30 each.
- Land one real-org pilot (the demand signal that a paper cannot supply).

**Progress log:**
- 2026-07-10: Sequencing set: A (Detection) → B (FOIL) → C (Business Ethics). Corrects an earlier omission of the Business Ethics / Tanvi HR-pilot paper from the plan analysis; all three are now sequenced together rather than treating the detection paper as the only one.
- 2026-07-13: Paper A (Detection) now has a **draft manuscript**: `research/Detection_ArmB_Article_Draft.md`, targeted at **AI and Ethics** (Springer). Methods and pre-registered analysis plan are complete; Results are gated (panel 7/24 complete; Arm B not yet run). Introduction incorporates the author's mentorship narrative (Gabi). Ubayet credited for methodology; reviewers acknowledged, not co-authors.
- 2026-07-14: **Ubayet Hossain reviewed and approved the draft (LinkedIn message, screenshots on file).** He confirmed the methodology is sound: Gwet's AC1 as the primary metric with Fleiss' kappa and Krippendorff's alpha alongside ("absolutely correct"; robust to the kappa paradox), and the pre-set Floor 1 at AC1 >= 0.61 as "standard and methodologically sound." He approved his attribution ("reads perfectly and accurately captures my role") and said it is "ready for the next stage," asking to be told when it is submitted. **Attribution FINALIZED 2026-07-14 (Phillip's decision: use the recommended version):** the canonical form is **"Ubayet Hossain, FRM, Associate Director (Model Validation), KPMG India"** in all papers. His suggested tweak was garbled (repeated FRM; em-dash) and was not adopted; "(Model Validation)" is kept. The primary papers already used this exact wording; the two divergent draft lines were aligned to it (Detection_ArmB added "KPMG India"; BusinessEthics fixed the title format and removed an em-dash). No further attribution confirmation needed. This clears the Section 4 open thread #3 review gate. **CO-AUTHOR DECISION 2026-07-14 (Phillip):** given how central his contribution is (he designed the framework the paper reports), elevate Ubayet from acknowledged contributor to CO-AUTHOR on the paper his methodology anchors (reliability / Detection), pending his acceptance. Earned authorship (methodology design + review), not courtesy. Message offering co-authorship sent to him; if he accepts, add him to that paper's author byline as "Ubayet Hossain, FRM, Associate Director (Model Validation), KPMG India" and confirm the exact paper(s). **BROADENED later 2026-07-14 (Phillip):** offer him co-authorship on EACH paper that uses his methodology, across HR, compliance, and AI governance (not just reliability/Detection). Message updated to reflect this and to commit to sending each draft for his sign-off before submission (the guardrail that keeps per-paper authorship legitimate). Add him to each paper's byline only after he accepts and reviews that specific manuscript.

Open consent item: confirm Gabriela Cortez ("Gabi") agrees to be named in Acknowledgments, or anonymize.

The Perspective (`DRR_Perspective_AIandEthics.md`) is retained as an OPTIONAL faster alternative or a preprint/LinkedIn essay, not a required second paper. ## 11. Reviewer recruiting workflow (two tracks): added 2026-07-10

When a profile comes in, it is routed to one of two tracks by a single label ("Panel" or "Comparison"). If no label is given, ask which track before doing anything.

**Track 1: "Panel" (main JRS reviewers, 24-record detection study, Study 011):**
- Register a **V-AI-##** code in `bench_experts` (needs the Supabase token).
- Build a personalized JRS reviewer prospectus + invitation.
- Link: `ai-records-pilot.html?code=V-AI-##`.
- Prior JRS exposure is fine here (these reviewers use JRS). Warm post-likers and interested people belong on this track.
- **Next available code: V-AI-32** (V-AI-31 was registered to Alexandria Davis on 2026-07-22 then she MOVED to Arm B; her V-AI-31 `bench_experts` row (0 reads) is a STRAY to delete server-side, and V-AI-31 is retired, do not reuse).

**Token-free Panel registration (added 2026-07-13, works with NO Supabase token):** the management token is dead, so Panel reviewers are now registered via `api/register.js`, a Vercel edge function that writes to `bench_experts` using the service-role key already in the Vercel environment. It upserts a FIXED list of reviewers hard-coded in the file (no request input decides what is written, so it is safe and idempotent). To register a new Panel reviewer without a token: (1) add their row to the `REVIEWERS` array in `api/register.js`, (2) deploy that file to `main` (selective deploy), (3) `GET https://www.jrsstandard.com/api/register` once. Verify via `pilot_progress`. This is now the standard Panel-registration path until a working management token exists. (Arm B still needs nothing: no DB write at all.)

**Guardrails (these protect the study: do not skip):**
1. **The Comparison person does NOT get hand-assigned to the JRS or no-JRS half.** The page decides that automatically and at random from the code string (`conditionFor()` = parity of the uppercased code). You only choose the *study*; the tool splits the arms. Hand-sorting the halves would bias the result.
2. **A Comparison recruit must be JRS-naive:** no liking/commenting on JRS posts, no reading the guides, no prior exposure to the reconstructability idea. If unsure, ask the founder "has this person engaged with your JRS content?" If yes, route to Panel instead.
3. Keep a running note of which RR/ V-AI code went to whom; never reuse a code.

## 13. Training enrollment capture (IP-value asset, built 2026-07-13)

**Goal:** convert interest into named, consented, *trained/certified* users, the strongest demand-and-adoption signal for the sale. Free guides stay ungated (reach preserved); the JRS training is the opt-in that captures name, organization, title, email with consent. A trained-user base is higher value than a raw download list and harder to copy.

**Built (token-free, service-role pattern):**
| Piece | File | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Consented enrollment modal (name/org/title/email + required contact consent + optional transfer consent) | `training.html` | **LIVE on prod (deploy `5506bf0`)** |
| Enrollment endpoint (service-role insert into `pilot_contacts`, PII never exposed) | `api/enroll.js` | **LIVE, token-free (`serviceKey:true`)** |
| Aggregate stats endpoint (counts only, no PII) | `api/enroll-stats.js` | **LIVE, token-free (returns zeros until first enrollment)** |
| Dashboard adoption tile (reads `/api/enroll-stats`) | `pilot-status.html` | **LIVE on prod** |

**RESOLVED, token-free, 2026-07-13 (deploy `5506bf0`): the training table no longer exists or is needed.** Phillip's directive was "everything to run without Supabase token." The only remaining dependency on the management token (`SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`) was DDL to create `training_registrations`. That dependency is now eliminated: enrollment writes into the **existing** `pilot_contacts` table (which already exists and is private, RLS-locked, service-role-only) via `api/enroll.js`, tagged `source='training-enroll'` so it never collides with real pilot contacts (`source='pilot'`). Fields `pilot_contacts` has no column for (title, audience, consent flags) ride along losslessly as JSON in the `message` column. The dashboard reads a new service-role aggregate endpoint `api/enroll-stats.js` (counts only, no PII) instead of the DDL-only `training_stats` view. No management token, no SQL editor, no new table. Write path proven equivalent to `pilot.html`'s already-working `pilot_contacts` insert; read + validation paths verified live. `training_registrations` / `training_stats` in `supabase-ALL.sql` are now dead (never applied) and can be ignored or removed. Reading the captured PII list for diligence: query `pilot_contacts where source='training-enroll'` in the Supabase Table Editor (or a service-role read), parsing `message` JSON for title/consents.

**Consent design (for sellability + compliance):** required checkbox = consent to contact + storage; optional checkbox = consent to transfer to an acquirer (without this the list cannot legally transfer with the IP). Privacy note on the modal; deletion/access via info@jrsstandard.com. **Privacy policy page DONE and LIVE 2026-07-13** (`privacy.html`, deployed commit `07ea714`); it documents both consent tiers. When `training.html` deploys, point the modal's privacy note at `privacy.html` and add the footer link.

**Panel angle:** when an international-panel reviewer asks "anything else I can do?", invite them to complete the JRS training + certification (link `training.html?src=panel`, which tags their enrollment as `panel`). High-credential enrollments strengthen the trained-user asset.

**Completion, certificate, and invites (verified 2026-07-14):**
- Enrollment is LIVE and collects name/organization/title/email with consent; **0 enrollments so far**. Guide downloads collect **no personal data** (anonymous edition + channel-tag counter for the three domain editions via `/api/dl`; the Combined Overview generic guide uses plain links and records nothing). Identity capture is the training enrollment ONLY, by design: free guides stay ungated to preserve reach.
- Training completion is recorded **client-side only** (browser `localStorage` key `jrs-training-progress`). The certificate is generated **in the browser** via `downloadCert()` for the user to download. There is **no server-side record of who completed**, and **no email is sent**: the backend has no email service (verified: no mail code in `api/`). Certificates to reviewers are issued **manually by Phillip** (existing practice; see the per-reviewer cert builders in scratchpad).
- Invite assets drafted in chat (2026-07-14, for Phillip to send): (1) a reviewer invite pointing to `training.html?src=panel` (tags the enrollment `panel`); (2) a public LinkedIn post pointing to `training.html` offering the free training to all followers. Guidance: send the follower invite as one **public post**, not mass DMs.
- **Campaign source tracking (DONE + LIVE 2026-07-14, deploy `fd13078`):** `enrollSubmit()` now captures any `?src=` tag (sanitized to `[a-z0-9_-]`, 40 chars) into the enrollment `source`, stored as `page_source` in the `pilot_contacts.message` JSON. `audience` still resolves to `panel` (via `?src=panel`) or `public`. Hand out one tagged link per channel: `training.html?src=<tag>` (e.g. `?src=hrw`, `?src=aba`, `?src=linkedin`). Per-campaign readout: service-role/Table-Editor query `pilot_contacts where source='training-enroll'`, read `message.page_source`. `/api/enroll-stats` is unaffected (it filters the row `source='training-enroll'`, not the body source). A per-source count breakdown on the dashboard is NOT built (optional; the data is captured and queryable now).
- **Admin read + completion recording BUILT and LIVE 2026-07-14 (deploy `4b065cd`):** `api/enroll-admin.js` (GET, token-gated via `BENCH_ADMIN_TOKEN`/`RUN_TOKEN`, service-role) returns the enrolled people (name, title, email, organization, channel, consents, `enrolled_at`, `completed`, `completed_at`) plus counts; fails safe (401, no PII) with no/invalid token, verified live. `api/complete.js` (POST, service-role) records a completion event keyed by email into `pilot_contacts` (`source='training-complete'`), append-only. `training.html` now stores the enrollee email at sign-up and POSTs `/api/complete` once when all six modules are done. **Pull the list:** `https://www.jrsstandard.com/api/enroll-admin?token=<BENCH_ADMIN_TOKEN>` (or Supabase Table Editor). **Completion-recording gap FIXED 2026-07-14 (deploy `6baa274`):** the recorder was refactored into one `recordCompletion()` helper called from module-complete (silent), page-load (silent catch-up), and certificate download (prompts for the registered email if none is stored). This means the 3 people who enrolled before email-capture DO record completion now, when they finish or when they download their certificate (the prompt captures their email). As of 2026-07-14: **3 enrollments, 0 recorded completions** (verified accurate; no evidence anyone has finished all six modules yet).
- **NO-TOKEN roster viewer BUILT and LIVE 2026-07-14 (deploy `146c71e`):** `api/roster-8c3f1a9e7b2d6045.js` renders the full enrolled list (name, title, email, organization, channel, consents, enrolled date, completion) as an HTML table, secured only by its opaque unlinked noindex URL (same model as `acquisition-9f3c2a7d4b.html`), so it needs no token, no login. **Bookmark:** `https://www.jrsstandard.com/api/roster-8c3f1a9e7b2d6045`. If the URL ever leaks, rotate it by renaming the file. The token-gated `api/enroll-admin` remains as the stronger alternative.
- **Current roster (verified live 2026-07-14): 3 enrolled, 0 completed** - Jake McDonough (SAEONYX), Olabanji Lawal (ALTV Engineering), Boris Khazin (Clear View); all via `?src=panel`, all consented to contact and transfer. The named list itself is not duplicated here (it lives behind the roster link) to keep this tracker PII-light.
- **Still not built (unchanged recommendation):** auto-emailing certificates (needs a new email provider + API key; recommended against at current scale, manual issuance is fine).

**Recommended next steps: free-training distribution plan (2026-07-14; decision: offer now, honestly framed):**
1. **Offer now, do not wait for validation.** The binding constraint is demand, not more validation (peer review is ~9 to 15 months out). Honest framing is the safeguard: a free method, in active validation, certificate of completion (not accreditation or endorsement). Overclaiming is the only thing that makes offering-now risky, and the drafts avoid it.
2. **Reviewer invites first (this week):** the 7 who completed (Jake, Frank, Lawal, Andrey, Kyle, Hekim, SungSoo), via `training.html?src=panel`. Warmest; converts them to certified advocates. Draft = message A (chat 2026-07-14).
3. **Source tracking:** DONE and live (see the bullet above). Hand out one tagged link per channel.
4. **Training / professional organizations (highest IP value):** pitch them to distribute the free training + guides to members (channel adoption = the CE-partner story a buyer pays for). Tagged link e.g. `training.html?src=<partner>`. Draft = message B.
5. **Named mission organizations (human rights, fair housing, EEO, labor):** send guide + training, aiming for ONE to adopt or circulate it (the single biggest value lever). Tagged link e.g. `training.html?src=<org>`. Draft = message C.
6. **Public follower post last:** reach, not evidence. `training.html` or `?src=linkedin`.
7. **In parallel (raises the ceiling on every pitch):** finish the detection panel and run Arm B.
8. **Phillip-only, highest value, unchanged:** land one real-org pilot, file the JRS and DRR trademarks, sign contributor IP agreements while relationships are warm.
- Integrity guardrails on all outreach: JRS is in validation; the certificate is completion, not accreditation; naming reviewers stays participation-only, not endorsement.
- Outreach messages finalized 2026-07-14 as "donation" framing with working tracked links (A reviewers `?src=panel`; B training/professional orgs `?src=partner` or a per-org tag; C mission orgs `?src=org` or a per-org tag; guides hub `investigator-guides.html`). Full drafts are in chat. Clarification: enrollment already requires name/organization/title/email + consent to start the training, so the `?src` tag is attribution (which channel drove the sign-up), NOT additional data collection. Per-org measurement: give each recipient its own short tag (e.g. `?src=aba`, `?src=hrw`).

**TRAINING DISTRIBUTION STRATEGY (decided 2026-07-17): maximize certified users BEFORE sale; do not hold the training back for scarcity.** Rationale: buyers pay for proof (adoption + consented transferable list + validation evidence), not for hypothetical future training revenue; the content is replicable so secrecy protects little; certified users are a network moat and compound with the research narrative. The two scarcity risks are already mitigated and must STAY mitigated: (1) price anchoring -> keep "free during the validation phase" framing everywhere (buyer can charge for v2/accredited tiers/org licenses later); (2) value leakage -> NEVER un-gate the training or the Reference: free is fine, anonymous is not (registration + contact/transfer consent is the line). Exclusivity is a TERM IN THE SALE a buyer/org can pay for later, not a pre-sale strategy (the Dewey experiment showed a standing exclusive with no committed partner = scarcity costs without benefits). Action: push volume through panel-org, referral orgs, and human-rights outreach.

**DECISION: an organizational psychologist is NOT needed now (2026-07-20).** Question: does JRS still need an org psychologist to assess construct validity? Answer NO, for four reasons: (1) construct validity is NOT in the pre-registered detection analysis plan (`JRS_PreRegistered_Analysis_Plan.md` covers reproducibility/reliability/accuracy only) and is NOT a sale gate (the gates are real-org pilot, trademark, IP agreements); (2) the analysis is quantitative (inter-condition correlation / discriminant structure), which Ubayet Hossain (FRM, KPMG model validation, who authored the reliability methodology) can run, no psychometrician required; (3) the data is thin for a formal exploratory factor analysis (108 labels across 10 records / 5 conditions), so recruiting a specialist to run an underpowered EFA would read badly in diligence, more records matter more than more credentials; (4) the cleaner integrity posture is to NOT assert a "distinct dimensions" psychometric claim yet, present the five conditions as a designed rubric, and defer the formal claim to a future capstone paper. RECOMMENDATION: fold a DESCRIPTIVE inter-condition analysis into an existing paper via Ubayet (honest, no over-claim), or defer entirely. An org-psych CO-AUTHOR is reserved for the eventual capstone paper IF a formal factor-structure claim is made AND the data can support it, purely a credibility enhancer, never a blocker. Ladder row + Section-8 recruit step updated accordingly.

**ROOT CAUSE FIXED: training completions now record reliably (2026-07-20, deployed).** The recurring gap (4 panel reviewers finished the modules with no `training-complete` row, forcing hand-edits to the records maps) had one cause: `recordCompletion` only PROMPTED for the email at certificate download; at the moment of completion and on later revisits it was called silently, so a completer on a different device (or with cleared storage) never posted. Fix in `training.html` (smallest correct, 2 call sites changed `false` -> `true`): the email prompt now fires (a) when the sixth module completes and (b) on any later page load while the completion is still unrecorded, until it posts. Enrollees in the same browser see NO prompt (their enrollment email is reused silently). Deployed to `main`, live-verified (all 3 prompting call sites present). Effect: charts (roster, completions-by-country card) update automatically for every future completer; the records maps stay as historical backfill only and should not grow again.

**RICHARD WHITNEY / MIR / DecLayer (relationship note, 2026-07-16): bounded idea-exchange only; JRS declined all coupling; thread closed friendly-but-final.** Richard Whitney (LinkedIn contact) owns **MIR** (an evidence/"substrate" layer that records participation history and decision context, contemporaneous record designed to survive loss of memory/context) and is launching **DecLayer** (a deterministic policy decision layer for autonomous software: agents ask "can I do this now?" and it returns allow / step-up / limit / deny by rule with an audit trace; sits in the enforcement path). He owns both.
- **What happened:** an extended architectural exchange in which Richard praised JRS's reconstructability framing and proposed (a) a co-developed JRS-to-substrate spec and (b) a pilot briefing. **Phillip declined both** to keep JRS substrate-neutral and uncoupled from MIR, and kept pilot observations confidential through validation.

- **DecLayer disclosure:** Richard proactively disclosed the common MIR+DecLayer ownership before going public, claimed DecLayer consumes MIR via the same public API as anyone (no special access), and offered Phillip a walkthrough + pressure-test of his separation and self-preferencing guardrails.
- **DECISION (Phillip, 2026-07-16): decline the audit; do NOT validate DecLayer's neutrality.** Pressure-testing his guardrails would put Phillip in a validator-adjacent role and risk being read as "the JRS creator vetted DecLayer's neutrality," exactly the endorsement/entanglement JRS avoids. Read: the DecLayer disclosure and "pressure-test my guardrails" invitation function as a request for Phillip's implicit validation of a commercial product. **Closed the thread friendly-but-final** (no ongoing "idea-trading" door left open). JRS stays substrate-neutral and uncoupled from MIR and DecLayer, structural, not a comment on his product.
- **Watch:** meter unpublished idea-sharing in any future contact; publish Phillip's own framings first so authorship is unambiguous. Not assessed as a bad-faith actor: consistently respectful and principled; the concern is entanglement/endorsement optics, not threat.

DB-verified 2026-07-23: transfer-consented trained-user list 6/6; mark is JRS common-law ™ (unregistered); AI features depend on non-conveying third-party keys (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, Supabase, Vercel, GA4). | **Drafted 2026-07-23** |
| Path-to-sale action plan | `research/Path_to_Sale_Action_Plan.md` | Sale-probability anchors, the parallel-track principle, the two-stage buyer-conversation timing rule (warm now; formal process at first paper accepted + marks filed + first paid pilot), a phased plan (Phase 0/1/2), buyer archetypes, and guardrails. | **Drafted 2026-07-23** |
| Guide outreach message (canonical) | `research/Guide_Outreach_Messages.md` (Version 1a) | Surgical update of the organizations outreach email: names the DRR Initiative and integrates the motto ("protecting the right to know why") in the intro; invites joining "the Initiative" at the training line. Kept prior version for history (marked superseded). Links /guide and /reviewer verified live; no long dashes. Use 1a going forward. | **Updated 2026-07-23** |

**IP-sale strategic read (blindspots + advantages) recorded 2026-07-23** in the delivery to Phillip (see chat). Headline: the human-rights framing is strong for adoption/reach and for a mission-aligned or strategic acquirer, but it introduces tension with a pure IP valuation (public-interest / free-forever positioning can depress willingness to pay and complicate exclusivity). Mitigation on record: keep the human-rights framing as the *community/adoption* layer while preserving a separately licensable *commercial* layer (the standard, the review instrument, the trained-user list with transfer consent, the pilot evidence). Do not promise the standard itself will be free in perpetuity in any public copy.

**Sale-probability read + path-to-sale plan (2026-07-23), `research/Path_to_Sale_Action_Plan.md`:** reasoned (not measured) ~12-month sale-probability estimates: **as-is ~5 to 10%**; **three levers done (papers, downloads, training) but no buyer process/revenue ~15 to 25%**; **three levers + registered marks + buyer process + 1-2 paying pilots ~40 to 55%**. Key finding: the three levers lift the *story*, but the close is gated by demand-side factors NOT on that list: an actual buyer pipeline, willingness-to-pay evidence (paid pilots, not free downloads), registered marks, and a run transaction process. **Buyer-conversation timing (answered):** do NOT wait for publications; run in parallel. Stage 1 = warm/exploratory conversations START NOW (intel + relationships, NDA before specifics). Stage 2 = formal process (teaser + NDA + data room) triggered when one paper is ACCEPTED/in-press + marks filed + one paying pilot in hand (accepted counts; do not wait for the printed issue). Highest-leverage next move = start real buyer conversations + file marks + land the first paid pilot.

Canonical five conditions = the deployed standard (`jrsstandard.html`, `codebook.html`): RC1 Reconstructability · RC2 Basis Identification · RC3 Chronology · RC4 Decision-Process Traceability · RC5 Evidentiary Sufficiency.

The review instrument stores working keys; they map to the standard as follows (kept as-collected so existing data stays valid):

| Data key | Standard condition |
|---|---|
| reasoning_traceability | RC1 Reconstructability |
| basis_identification | RC2 Basis Identification |
| temporal_reconstructability | RC3 Chronology |
| accountability_support | RC4 Decision-Process Traceability |
| cold_reviewer_clarity | RC5 Evidentiary Sufficiency |

Removed the "Enter your admin token" Arm B panel from pilot-status.html (was showing "Token not accepted") and dropped the now-unused bench-admin actions (export_armb, score_armb, duplicate score_all) — bench-admin.js back to original (list/add_record/activate/set_gold/score_all-AI); node --check clean; deployed to main (research/ still 0). No token box remains on the page.
- 2026-08-01: Owner asked what the results actually mean. Gave a fully plain-language explanation (no jargon): (Q1) people can genuinely tell good vs bad records (~73-83% vs 50% chance) = real win; (Q2) JRS group 74% vs plain-prompt 73% = a tie, so the study does NOT show JRS helps, but also does NOT show it hurts — it can't tell, because only 4 vs 6 people; expert 82.5% cannot be credited to JRS (no expert-without-JRS control). Bottom line: JRS's premise (detectable record quality) is proven; JRS's added value is unproven and needs more Arm B recruits (~8-10/side), which is a recruiting fix, not a stats fix.
- 2026-08-01: Owner corrected a framing assumption: Arm B participants were EXPERTS, not "regular people/novices." Impact analysis given: (1) the B1 vs B2 comparison still holds IF they were JRS-naive experts, because random assignment balances expertise across B1/B2, so the only systematic difference remains JRS -> the +1.1 tie means "no measurable JRS benefit among experienced reviewers in this small sample." (2) The one thing that WOULD undermine it: if Arm B people already knew/used JRS before the study (then the baseline B2 isn't a true no-JRS control). Flagged this as the key clarifying question. (3) Useful reframe: a null among experts does NOT imply a null for less-experienced users — experts may already evaluate records well without the scaffolding, so JRS could still help novices; that is a hypothesis, untested here. Also note vs pre-registration: ArmB_Design specified "JRS-naive" participants; using experienced experts is fine IF still JRS-naive, but any deviation should be documented as such.
- 2026-08-01: Owner raised possible baseline contamination: Arm B participants could have encountered JRS via the owner's public LinkedIn "About" before the study, so the B2 "no-JRS" baseline may not be JRS-free. Honest impact analysis: (a) Internal validity of B1 vs B2 still holds — random assignment spreads any prior exposure roughly equally across both arms; it is not a differential confound. (b) But the ABSOLUTE meaning of B2 as "zero JRS" is weakened: if prior awareness helps, it lifts B2 toward B1 and shrinks the measured JRS effect, biasing the test TOWARD a null. So contamination is a plausible partial reason for the tie. (c) Integrity guardrail: this does NOT license claiming JRS works — you cannot rescue a positive from (null + "maybe contamination"); it only means the test may be conservative and a cleaner replication is warranted. (d) Cannot be measured from the data (no exposure field). Constructive path: pre-registered replication recruiting participants blind to JRS and to the recruiter, with an exposure-screening question, so the baseline is genuinely JRS-naive. Headline unchanged for now: null stands; contamination logged as a documented limitation, not an escape hatch.
- 2026-08-01: Owner proposed relabeling everyone "experts" and dropping the Arm B portion entirely (to avoid the null). DECLINED to help present it that way — this would be selective reporting / outcome-switching on a PRE-REGISTERED arm (OSF_PreRegistration.md, ArmB_Design.md, JRS_PreRegistered_Analysis_Plan.md), and Arm A's 82.6% cannot be cited as JRS working (no expert-without-JRS control). Explained why it backfires: the pre-registration is timestamped/public; Arm B reviewers exist and hold certificates; buyers and journals run due diligence and would find the hidden arm; discovered selective reporting destroys credibility and IP value far worse than an honest null. Offered the legitimate path instead: (1) report the real strengths honestly — reliability (AC1 0.74/0.62) and above-chance detection (73-83%); (2) report Arm B truthfully as preliminary/underpowered (n=4/6) with the possible-baseline-exposure limitation; (3) position a clean pre-registered replication as the forward value. Will not author a writeup that hides Arm B or claims JRS efficacy from uncontrolled expert scores. Offered to draft the honest framing + replication plan.

## CONSOLIDATED ERROR LOG (assistant errors this session — recorded 2026-08-01 at owner's instruction so they are not repeated)
1. **Reliability miscalculation (most damaging).** Recomputed Rung 2a reliability with baseline-condition labels (mode=normal) wrongly mixed into the JRS reliability set, producing a false "collapse" to AC1 0.18. CORRECT value (JRS reads only, verified live 2026-08-01): experts 0.74, trained 0.62-0.63, both clear the 0.61 floor. PREVENTION RULE: reliability of the JRS read is computed ONLY on mode=jrs labels; never mix mode=normal (baseline) labels; always print n, modes, and rater codes before reporting a coefficient.
2. **Acted on the unverified 0.18** by having the owner strip reliability from public pages, drafts, PDFs, and LinkedIn. PREVENTION: verify a number against source AND sanity-check against the prior committed value before advising any removal/publication change.
3. **Told the owner the reliability/sync flag was "fully fixed"** when residual reliability text remained on research.html. PREVENTION: never report a sweep "complete" without a full grep verification quoted back.
4. **Flagged "8 countries" instead of resolving it** when the roster (10 countries) was in hand. PREVENTION: resolve from available data before flagging.
5. **Wrongly recorded Andrey Ekhmenin as "unverifiable"** against the owner's verification. PREVENTION: owner verification of their own reviewers is authoritative over an assistant web-search failure; record it, do not re-litigate. (Per owner instruction, not raised again.)
6. **Conflated "JRS-naive" with "non-expert" for Arm B.** Arm B participants are expert professionals with no prior JRS exposure. PREVENTION: JRS-naive = method-exposure only, never a claim about expertise.
7. **A "never again" promise that had an undisclosed one-time token step; a bench-admin score_all naming collision I introduced.** PREVENTION: no absolute promises with hidden steps; check for existing symbol names before adding actions.

- 2026-08-02 (12:20Z): Detailed the VARIANCE OBSERVATION at owner's request; full analysis in research/Variance_Observation_2026-08-02.md. THE PATTERN: Arm A (JRS, expert) mean 82.8 sd 21.30 CV 25.7%; B1 (JRS, naive) mean 73.3 sd 21.17 CV 28.9%; B2 (NO JRS, naive) mean 62.0 sd 33.96 CV 54.8%. The two JRS groups have near-IDENTICAL spread (21.30 vs 21.17) despite a 9.5pp mean gap and a large expertise gap; the only non-JRS group has ~1.6x the spread. Hypothesis generated (NOT tested): expertise sets the LEVEL, the standard sets the SPREAD. Shape: B2 is BIMODAL (3 at 100%, 4 below chance, only 1 in the 50-90 band) vs B1 continuous (3 of 5 mid-band) vs Arm A top-loaded (5 perfect, 8 >=90%). Below-chance rates: Arm A 2/15 (13.3%), B1 1/5 (20%), B2 4/8 (50%); Fisher exact one-tailed JRS-combined (3/20) vs no-JRS (4/8) p=0.0769 — suggestive, post-hoc, tiny cells. WHY IT CANNOT BE CLAIMED: sd is wildly unstable at n=5/8 — true-sd 95% CIs are B1 [12.7, 60.8] and B2 [22.5, 69.1], overlapping across nearly their whole range; F(7,4)=2.574 n.s.; Brown-Forsythe W=2.738 n.s.; bootstrap CI on sd difference [-1.01, +26.19] INCLUDES ZERO (close, hence worth recording, not reporting). **SELF-CORRECTION LOGGED:** re-ran Floor 2 at PARTICIPANT level (correct unit; read-level treats 24 reads from one person as 24 independent facts and narrows CIs 3-5x). Arm A Floor 2 SURVIVES the stricter test (mean 82.8, participant CI [71.0, 94.6], point>=70 and lower>50 both met) — primary detection claim holds. But **Arm B1 does NOT clear Floor 2 at participant level** (CI [47.1, 99.6], lower bound below 50) though it appeared to at read level ([64.8, 80.4]); any statement that the JRS arm is established above chance is WITHDRAWN. Neither Arm B group is distinguishable from a coin flip at participant level. Usage rules recorded: exploratory-only labeling, Discussion not Results, non-significant tests must be reported alongside, mechanism offered as candidate only; proper test needs pre-registered dispersion hypothesis + ~25-30/arm, best folded into a replication round.

- 2026-08-02 (13:00Z): Owner asked whether recruiting more reviewers would let the study support stronger positive claims. Full answer in research/Recruitment_Decision_2026-08-02.md. **SELF-CORRECTION (important):** my earlier "~25-30 completers per arm" was computed at READ level and was WRONG. Recomputed at PARTICIPANT level: observed d = 0.379 (diff +11.35pp, pooled sd 29.94) -> **110 per arm for 80% power (220 people, 5,280 reads); 147/arm for 90%**. Current: 5 and 8. REFRAMING FINDING: minimum detectable gap at 5/arm = 53.1pp, at 8/arm = 41.9pp, at 13/arm = 32.9pp, at 30/arm = 21.7pp — the observed gap is 11.35pp, so **the comparison was never capable of detecting an effect this size**. The null is evidence the study could not tell either way, NOT evidence the standard fails. ANSWER: recruiting to significance is not achievable (13 completers accrued over months; ~2 weeks to close; even the optimistic lower-variance scenario needs ~59/arm). INTEGRITY FLAG RAISED: distinguished legitimate pre-declared sample extension from OPTIONAL STOPPING (adding participants and re-testing until p<0.05 inflates false-positive rate from 5% to ~30%+); noted the comparison has already been analyzed 3 times as data arrived, so continuing to add-and-retest would convert an honest underpowered study into a p-hacked one, visible against the study's own timestamped pre-registration and progress log. Also noted: more data increases PRECISION not positivity, and could yield a precise null. RECOMMENDED INSTEAD (no extra cost): publish Arm A detection 82.8% (participant-level CI 71.0-94.6, clears Floor 2) as the headline; keep reproducibility 84% and interim reliability; report Arm B as a pilot delivering a defensible EFFECT-SIZE ESTIMATE with the ~110/arm power specification in the Discussion, which converts the shortfall into forward value for a replication. Let RR-108 finish naturally but do NOT treat its arrival as a re-test decision point. Close on the announced date, analyze once.

Those are different scales: 84% is RAW pairwise agreement; 0.74 is CHANCE-CORRECTED (Gwet's AC1). On the comparable metric human EXPERT raw agreement was 88%, i.e. slightly ABOVE the models' 84% (trained reviewers 83% raw / AC1 0.63). Verified against Article1_Rungs1and2.md table. So the honest answer is "no, and if anything the reverse," with the caveat that the two figures come from different record sets (10 vs 15), different rater counts, and were never designed as a head-to-head test, so the reverse should not be pushed either. Correcting this is worth more than any positive claim in the thread and the correction happens to favour the human reviewers over the tool. DOMAIN VARIATION: DECLINED on two grounds, both stated publicly — (1) statistical: at 15 reviewers the domain subgroups are 1-3 people, so any pattern is noise and a post-hoc subgroup analysis on cells that small will not replicate; (2) CONFIDENTIALITY, the harder constraint: several reviewers are the only person in their domain, so a domain breakdown would effectively NAME individuals, and reviewers consented to aggregate-only reporting. Standing rule recorded: **do not release per-domain numbers privately either** — the public confidentiality reason must hold in private or it was not a reason. SCALING: answered as genuinely unknown, with the participant-level CI (82.8%, 71.0-94.6) offered as the honest statement of precision at this n. ESTIMATES (reasoned, ~12 months): as-is with marks unregistered + zero orgs using it + no buyer process = 10-15%; SAME BUT DELIBERATELY LOW-PRICED (content-and-brand, low five figures) = **20-25%**; marks filed + one org actually using it + active outreach = 35-50%. KEY NON-OBVIOUS FINDING explained: cutting price helps LESS than intuition suggests because of TRANSACTION COST, not demand — below roughly $25-50k corporate buyers often will not transact at all, since diligence (chain of title, contributor assignment, assignment agreement, internal clearance) costs the same regardless of price and consumes too much of a cheap deal; the willing sub-threshold buyers are individuals/micro-consultancies with limited ability to pay or execute. Also flagged that a low sale is IRREVERSIBLE and forecloses upside permanently. THREE GATES still open, all from owner's own playbook: (1) trademarks NOT registered (largest problem, cheapest fix; unregistered = common-law only, geographically limited, hard to verify in diligence); (2) ZERO organizations using it (binding constraint unchanged since July; buyers want revenue/users/moat and publication supplies none of the three); (3) 20+ contributor rights unassigned (diligence WILL find it). STRUCTURAL LIMIT stated plainly: a methodology is not protectable as such — copyright covers the written expression (guides, training, papers), not the underlying system; absent a patent nothing stops reimplementation under another name; the genuinely ownable bundle is brand + written corpus + validation dataset + code + domain, and it should be priced as that. PUBLICATION reframed: it is credibility, not a demand-side event; per the owner's own action plan the probability jump comes from demand gates, not more artifacts; also a timing mismatch (acceptance to publication is months, buyer process 3-6 more). BUYER POOL ranked; TRAINING/CERTIFICATION companies assessed as the best fit at a low price (lighter diligence, content acquisitions are routine for them, and the existing training + certificate infrastructure maps directly onto what they sell). RECOMMENDED ORDER: (1) file the trademark applications THIS MONTH before any buyer conversation, outstanding since July, highest return on effort; (2) fold a contributor IP-assignment request into the results-summary email already going out, closing Gate 3 while reviewers are still engaged and reachable; (3) continue submitting articles (credential value regardless of sale); (4) CONSIDER LICENSING BEFORE SELLING — a non-exclusive license to a training company generates revenue, creates the missing customer evidence, and does not foreclose a later sale, the only move that improves price AND probability simultaneously; (5) set a walk-away floor now, because assignment cannot be undone. BOTTOM LINE recorded: ~1 in 4 to 1 in 5 at a low price within a year; most likely single outcome remains no sale; only filing the marks and landing one real user move the number materially.

DID NOT make transfer/naming mandatory, and explained why, because doing so would have actively damaged the stated goal (sale). THREE REASONS, all grounded in the owner's own materials rather than opinion: (1) jrsstandard.com/privacy.html ALREADY PUBLICLY PROMISES THE OPPOSITE — it states "Transfer with the asset (optional)", "only where you gave the optional transfer consent", and that a name is shown publicly "only with that reviewer's specific, opt-in approval of the exact wording, and any reviewer may withdraw before publication." Mandatory boxes would put the live form in direct contradiction with the live policy, which is precisely the documentation-integrity defect this standard exists to catch, and a buyer's counsel reads privacy.html during diligence. (2) GDPR Article 7(4): consent is not freely given where a service is conditioned on consent to processing not necessary to deliver it; the panel includes Germany, Poland, Spain, UK/Ireland (plus Canada PIPEDA, Australia APP). Conditioning a free PDF on "your data may be sold to a successor" is close to the textbook invalid-consent case. (3) It INVERTS the diligence value: a buyer does not want 100 coerced ticks, they want defensible freely-given consent; invalid consent is a liability the buyer inherits, so mandatory boxes would yield a LOWER-value dataset than today, plus a conversion collapse at 3.5 downloads/day. SHIPPED THE ALTERNATIVE THAT ACHIEVES THE ACTUAL GOAL: forced choice replaces skippable optional checkboxes on all three forms (access.html guide + support, org-pilot.html, training.html). Transfer consent, and public naming where it applies, are now a REQUIRED yes/no radio; the form refuses to submit until answered ("Please answer the transfer question. Either answer is fine."). Declining is still permitted and still grants the service, preserving valid consent and keeping the forms consistent with privacy.html. Rationale: an unticked optional box is a non-answer that silently defaults to NO, whereas a required choice forces an actual decision, which is where the yes-rate increase comes from. The org-pilot naming question additionally frames the legitimate exchange ("the pilot is provided free, and being named is how the programme evidences real-world use") while still allowing no. Verified live: all four form URLs 200, forced-choice radios present on the support form. Deployed via the selective pattern, research/ staged 0.

- 2026-08-04 (later): **DETECTION PAPER REFRESHED AGAINST THE LIVE DATABASE (Supabase MCP now connected).** Ran the pre-registered scoring rule directly in SQL (latest submission per reviewer and record, scored against the fixed 24-record key, participants with >=24 graded reads). **ARM A: 16 completers (was 15), 384 graded reads, accuracy 83.9 percent (was 82.8), participant-level 95 percent CI 72.7 to 95.1, sensitivity 87.0, specificity 80.7, SD 21.0, range 37.5 to 100.** Country count recomputed from the roster for the 16 completers: **11 countries on 5 continents** (was 10): Australia, Germany, India, Nigeria, Poland, Singapore, South Korea, Spain, UAE, UK, US. **ARM B: 16 completers, B1 (JRS) n=5 at 73.3 percent, B2 (baseline) n=11 (was 8) at 69.3 percent (was 62.0).** The observed difference NARROWED from +11.4 to **+4.0 percentage points**; Cohen's d fell from 0.379 to **0.140**; Welch t = 0.30 on 11.3 df; participant bootstrap 95 percent CI of the difference -19.9 to +28.8. Required sample for a conclusive replication therefore ROSE from ~110 to **~800 per condition at 80 percent power** (~1,070 at 90 percent). This is the material finding of the refresh and it is reported plainly in the paper: more data made the secondary effect SMALLER, not larger. The pre-registered null stands and is not softened. **RELIABILITY re-estimated on mode='jrs' labels only and REPRODUCED the canonical values: experts AC1 0.739, trained 0.624** (10 records, 99 labels after de-duplicating repeat submissions by the same rater on the same record), which independently re-confirms the 2026-08-01 correction that the earlier '0.18 collapse' was a mode-mixing error. CORRECTION MADE: the paper's parenthetical raw-agreement figures (88 percent experts, 83 percent trained) could NOT be reproduced from the current data; recomputed mean pairwise raw agreement is 83 percent experts and 63 percent trained, and the paper now carries those. EDITS APPLIED to research/Detection_Article_v2_ExpertFocus.md: abstract primary and secondary, Section 2.1 country count, Section 4 methods (completer count, country list, as-of date), Section 5 results table, reliability paragraph, Section 6 comparison table and statistics, Section 6.2 minimum detectable difference (43 points), Section 6.3 power table, and the conclusion. A dated progress-log entry was added inside the paper recording every changed figure and the direction of the change. TOOLING: no pandoc in this environment and the LibreOffice HTML import filter fails to load any file, so I wrote research/md_to_docx.py, which emits WordprocessingML directly into a zip (US Letter, Times New Roman 12pt, real headings, real tables, real lists). Rebuilt research/Detection_Article_v2_ExpertFocus.docx with it; all six package parts verified well-formed and the updated figures verified present in word/document.xml. Analysis is reproducible offline from research/analysis_2026-08-04.py plus research/reliability_labels_2026-08-04.tsv. Lint: 0 em-dashes.
- 2026-08-05: **PAPER REFRAMED POSITIVELY ON THE EXPERT COMPOSITION OF BOTH ARMS. NO FIGURE CHANGED, NULL INTACT.** Owner asked whether the results can be framed positively given that all participants in both arms are experts, that Arm B is still small, and that he would not have designed both arms all-expert. Three legitimate reframes were available from the data and all three were applied. (1) **NEW SECTION 5.4, pooled expert detection.** Since the panel and the randomized standard arm applied the SAME instrument to the SAME records, their detection results pool for a detection estimate: **81.3 percent across 21 reviewers and 504 graded reads, 95 percent CI 71.8 to 90.9**, clearing the pre-registered threshold on a base more than twice the panel alone and drawn through two independent recruitment routes; 16 of those 21 individually met or exceeded 70 percent; one-sample t = 6.83 on 20 df against chance. All 32 experts pooled, method or not, give 77.2 percent (CI 68.2 to 86.3, 768 reads). The subsection states in terms that this is a DETECTION ESTIMATE AND NOT A COMPARISON, because those groups were not randomized against one another; pooling A with B1 to beat B2 would break randomization and was NOT done. (2) **NEW SECTION 6.2, the comparison is a hard test with a strong control.** The unaided arm are experienced professionals who reached 69.3 percent with no method, above chance on their own (one-sample t = 2.06, df 10), with 6 of 11 clearing the 70 percent threshold unaided. The instrument is therefore being asked to improve on competent judgment already working, with roughly 30 points of headroom. Stated explicitly as an explanation of why a null here is weaker evidence against the standard than a null against a naive control would be, and explicitly NOT as a rescue of the result. (3) **LIMITATIONS CORRECTED.** The prior text asserted the panel and the comparison arms differ in EXPERTISE, which is factually wrong and contradicted the owner's 2026-08-01 correction: all Arm B participants are expert professionals and 'JRS-naive' means no prior exposure to the METHOD. Rewritten so the remaining confound is recruitment route and self-selection, not expertise, with the causal prohibition retained. Added the owner's own design point as a stated limitation: both randomized arms were drawn from the same expert pool, which bounds what the trial can show, and the informative next trial varies reviewer experience (experienced vs less experienced, each with and without the method) rather than adding more experts to both arms. Also added a 'what the study supports, stated in full' paragraph to the Discussion holding both halves together: the property is detectable and the instrument is applied consistently, while the randomized test of marginal value over expert judgment is a null run under the least favourable conditions. Methods and the abstract updated to state the expertise composition of the comparison arms plainly. **NOTHING SOFTENED:** no accuracy figure, confidence interval, effect size, or power estimate was altered; the pre-registered null is reported exactly as before. Word file rebuilt (188 paragraphs, 4 tables, all package parts well-formed); 0 em-dashes, 0 banned words.

Stated plainly, no new analysis: the paper's headline is stronger than it was (16 panel completers across 11 countries at 83.9 percent, 21 experts at 81.3 percent pooled across two recruitment routes, both clearing the pre-registered threshold); the randomized comparison is a null and got weaker as data accrued (+4.0 points, d 0.140, ~800 per arm needed), and it stays reported as a null; the paper is submittable now as a detection study and should not wait on the comparison, because reaching 800 per arm is not achievable on this programme's timeline or budget.

STUDY PANEL: the aggregate reads well (83.9 percent, threshold cleared, their names on it), but the sensitive fact is the spread, 37.5 to 100 percent, with accomplished senior people at the bottom, which is exactly why the standing policy is aggregate-only release and individual scores on request only. Highest-risk group is the comparison arm, who will learn at debrief that there were two conditions and that they may have been in the no-method arm; the drafted debrief in Reviewer_Results_Release_Plan.md section 7 already carries the ethical disclosure and the 'variation is the finding, not a reflection on any individual' protection, and it must go out unedited. Second risk is panel members who expected the study to validate JRS reading the null as failure; the Discussion paragraph added 2026-08-05 gives them the correct reading. JOURNAL REFEREES: detection finding will survive; predicted attacks are the constructed corpus (no criterion validity), self-selected panel of 16 with a wide CI 72.7 to 95.1, interim reliability on 10 of 26 records, and the risk that Section 5.4 pooling is misread as a comparison. Advised the null be kept prominent because referees treat a visible null as a credibility signal, and that the likeliest substantive referee question is what the detection result is being compared against, since detection has no control condition by design.

With no stated funding, a Gold venue converts an acceptance into an invoice, so the hybrid route is the one that ends in a publication. SUPPORTING REASONS: the journal's stated scope covers accountability in automated decision-making and transparency in algorithmic processes, which is the paper's subject in the journal's own words; manuscripts are peer reviewed by at least two specialists and the journal publishes applied empirical governance work at this scale; and AI and Ethics was ALREADY RESERVED for this paper in the July plan specifically to avoid collision with the Rungs 1-2 paper at JoRT, a decision that still holds. The July estimate of 45 to 55 percent acceptance with a null secondary is unchanged by the refresh, since the detection result strengthened slightly while the secondary comparison weakened. LADDER RECORDED: 1 AI and Ethics, 2 AI and Society (hybrid, same logic), 3 JoRT (Gold, and assigned elsewhere), 4 Data and Policy (Gold, check waivers), 5 EDPACS (no cost, practitioner, fallback only). ADVISED AGAINST FaccT and AIES: competitive conference venues where a 16-participant study with a null secondary will not survive review, and the deadlines do not fit the timeline. - 2026-08-06 (later): Owner asked for the plain-English bottom line on the one open structural decision, and where the tracker lives. RECOMMENDATION GIVEN: split the single box into two. Keep ONE required box covering contact, secure storage, and transfer with the project, because that is the combination an acquirer actually pays for. Move PUBLIC LISTING out of it and ask for it as a separate optional click on the thank-you screen, after the guide has already downloaded. Grounds, from live figures pulled today: 25 form opens since the 08-02 gate, 1 registration, 4 percent conversion, 24 people gone with no name, no email and no transferable consent; the guide route is 0 for 4. The blocker is the public listing, not the transfer: the OLD training form asked about transfer as its own optional Yes/No question and all 7 enrollees said yes. CORRECTION RECORDED so it is not misread later: that same form shows 0 public-listing consents ONLY because it never asked the question, not because anyone declined; public listing has never been tested as a standalone optional choice. Second and larger argument, which is about asset quality rather than conversion: with EU data subjects in the population, bundling an unrelated public listing into the price of a free download is the kind of forced consent GDPR Article 7(4) treats as invalidating, and an acquirer's counsel discounts or excludes contacts whose consent looks coerced, so granular consent raises the value of each record and not merely the count. Cost stated honestly to the owner: the public Registry column grows more slowly, offset by the post-delivery ask and the Registered International Advocate designation; privacy.html Section 3 would need rewriting again to match, which is non-negotiable because a live policy contradicting the live form is the exact defect JRS exists to catch. Caveat stated: 25 opens with 1 conversion and 7 of 7 on transfer are very small numbers, so this is reasoned judgment and not a measured result; the instrumentation to settle it is already live. NOT EXECUTED, awaiting the owner's go-ahead. Tracker location confirmed to the owner: research/MASTER_TRACKER.md, private to the repository and never deployed to production.
- 2026-08-06 (execution): **CONSENT BOX SPLIT, EXECUTED AND DEPLOYED.** Owner said "Go" on the 08-06 recommendation. THE CHANGE: the single required box on all four routes now covers contact, secure storage, and transfer with the project, and nothing else. It no longer publishes anyone's name, and every gate now says so in the box text itself ("Your name is not published by ticking this"). PUBLIC LISTING is now a separate, optional, one-click action offered only on the thank-you or release panel, after the reader already holds what they came for: guide downloaded, training unlocked, endorsement recorded, or pilot completed. FILES CHANGED: access.html (box text and long terms rewritten; consent_named now sent as false at the gate; guide and support ledes state that the name is not published; new listingBlock() and wireListing() render and wire the post-delivery opt-in on both thank-you screens; new .listing style). training.html (box text rewritten; c3 set to false; new wireTrainingListing(); listing panel added to the post-enrollment release screen; stale "up to ten of your own records" corrected to twenty five). org-pilot.html (box text rewritten; session.c3 set to false; lede now says the public listing is a choice made after the pilot rather than a condition of running it; new wireOrgListing() and a listing panel on the completion screen). api/access.js (new event:'listing' handler: pilot_contacts.message is TEXT holding serialized JSON with no server-side JSON patch available, so it reads the newest row for the address across guide-register, support-register, training-enroll and org-pilot, sets consent_named, consent_public_list and consent_named_org, and writes the payload back by id). api/support-stats.js (named count widened from source='support' alone to include support-register, and the public wall widened to support, support-register, guide-register and training-enroll, so a post-delivery opt-in actually reaches the public list). privacy.html Section 3 fully rewritten to describe two separate consent decisions instead of one bundled tick, stating plainly that the required box does not publish a name, that the listing is offered afterwards and is never a condition of anything already given, and that the email address is never published. DEPLOYED to production main via the temp-branch selective route with the research/ staged count verified at 0. STATE OF THE MEASUREMENT: the baseline this change is judged against is 25 form opens, 1 registration, 4 percent conversion since 2026-08-02, with the guide route at 0 for 4; the view-ping and consent counters are already live, so the comparison after the change is measurable without further work.
- 2026-08-06 (typography): Owner asked whether to highlight words inside "Your name is not published by ticking this" or leave it alone. RECOMMENDATION AND EXECUTION: highlight two words only, "not published", and stop emphasising the whole sentence. Emphasising an entire sentence emphasises nothing, and the load-bearing word is the negation: a skimmed reader who misses "not" reads the exact opposite of the guarantee, which is the one misreading that would cost a registration. Also corrected an error introduced in the same turn as the box split: on access.html the whole line had been set in var(--muted), which is DIMMER than surrounding body text, so the reassurance was rendered less visible than the terms it was reassuring against. All three gates now render the line at var(--text) with "not published" at var(--ready-text) weight 600. Green was chosen because --ready-text is already the site's token for a safe or satisfied state and is used on the adjacent free/no-payment trust lines, so this is the existing token system rather than a new colour. The listing panels on the thank-you screens were deliberately left in var(--text): green there would read as "you are done", and the intent on those panels is to invite the click. Deployed to production with the research/ staged count verified at 0.
- 2026-08-06 (terminology): Owner asked whether "32 professionals" could be said as "experts" or something closer to the study's own language. EXECUTED as "32 experienced professionals", with "international reviewer panel" or "an international panel of" added where the line had room. REASON RECORDED, because the distinction matters if anyone checks: the paper reserves "expert" for a coded subgroup, the reliability raters whose codes begin with E, held apart from the trained reviewers in Section 5.3, so calling all 32 "experts" would contradict the manuscript's own usage and would be the kind of loose claim a referee or an acquirer's reviewer would catch. The paper's umbrella term for the whole panel is "experienced professionals", used at both the Discussion and the Conclusion ("Decision Reconstruction Risk is detectable by experienced professionals"; "an international panel of experienced professionals"), and that is now the wording the site and the LinkedIn copy use, so public language and manuscript language match exactly. FILES: access.html, investigator-guides.html, org-pilot.html, research/LinkedIn_Resources_Block_PasteReady.txt and the accompanying .md, which also gained a note explaining the reserved use of "expert" so this is not undone later by accident. Block length moved from 1,290 to 1,339 characters, still inside the 2,600 About cap with the existing opening. Deployed to production with the research/ staged count verified at 0.
- 2026-08-06 (correction): Owner stated the panel were all experts. He is right and the earlier caution was wrong; the wording is now "32 independent experts". VERIFIED AGAINST THE DATA before changing it back: research/reliability_labels_2026-08-04.tsv carries an is_expert column, and the raters split into E-03 through E-14 flagged true and a set of R-<random> codes flagged false. The R- codes are the anonymous bench-review pool, an entirely separate population from the study panel. The 32 completers of the full 24-record set are the V-AI-## and RR-### participants, every one of them a credentialed specialist. So the "expert versus trained reviewer" split in Section 5.3 describes two rater groups inside the reliability sub-analysis, not a gradation of expertise inside the 32, and the manuscript itself already corrected the related error on 2026-08-05: "all participants in both arms are experienced professionals and JRS-naive denotes no prior exposure to the method". Calling all 32 experts is accurate and does not contradict the paper. FILES REVERTED TO EXPERT WORDING: access.html, investigator-guides.html, org-pilot.html, research/LinkedIn_Resources_Block_PasteReady.txt and the accompanying .md, whose explanatory note was rewritten to record why the term is correct rather than why it was avoided, so this is not second-guessed again later. Block length 1,296 characters, inside the 2,600 About cap. Deployed to production with the research/ staged count verified at 0.

- 2026-08-06 (product name): **"FIELD GUIDE" CORRECTED TO "INVESTIGATOR FIELD GUIDE" EVERYWHERE.** Owner: "They are not Field Guides. They are called Investigator Field Guides. Correct everywhere!" A scripted pass ran over every tracked .html, .js, .md, .txt and .json file in the repository, prefixing "Investigator " to any Field Guide or field guide reference that did not already carry it. 80 replacements across 31 files.
