- 2026-08-06 (participant count): **"32" UNDERCOUNTED THE PROGRAMME AND HAS BEEN REVISED TO 48 ACROSS THREE STUDIES.** Owner sent a screenshot of the live registry gate and said there are definitely more than 32, and to check every study. He is right. EVERY ANON-READABLE STUDY VIEW WAS QUERIED, not just the two the figure came from, and a reproducible script is now filed at research/count_participants.py so this number never has to be reconstructed from memory again. FINDINGS: Study 011, the detection panel (Arm A, V-AI-## codes), 27 registered and 16 completing a full 24-record set. Study 012, the randomized comparison (Arm B, RR-### codes), 17 registered and 16 completing a full 24-record set, splitting 5 in arm B1 and 11 in arm B2. Study 004, reviewer reliability, 24 distinct raters submitting 124 labels on the shared record set, comprising 8 E- coded experts and 16 bench reviewers. Registered participant codes across all three studies: 68. WHY THE OLD FIGURE WAS WRONG: 32 counts only people who completed a full 24-record set, which is an instrument that exists in Studies 011 and 012 only. Study 004 uses a different instrument with a different record set and different codes, so its 24 raters were invisible to the figure entirely, and the site was crediting two thirds of the programme. WHY 48 AND NOT 56: participant codes are issued per study and the anon views carry no name for the reliability raters, so cross-study identity cannot be resolved from here. The manuscript states Study 012 has "its own participants, its own recruitment, its own participant codes", so Arm A and Arm B are disjoint. The 8 E- raters are described in the manuscript as experts and MAY be panel members, so they are excluded from the published headline entirely; the 16 bench reviewers are a separately recruited pool and are counted. 48 is therefore a floor that stays true even in the worst case where every E- rater is also a panel member. The upper bound if no overlap exists is 56, and that figure is recorded here but is deliberately NOT published. WORDING SHIPPED, which keeps the strongest per-person claim intact rather than replacing it: "48 reviewers across three studies have graded records for this work, including 32 independent experts across 11 countries who each completed a full 24-record set." FILES: access.html, investigator-guides.html, org-pilot.html, research.html (Evidence Base card now opens with the programme-wide figure), research/LinkedIn_Resources_Block_PasteReady.txt and the accompanying .md, whose social-proof note now records the floor reasoning and points at the script. FINGERPRINTS REMOVED, named so the same tells are not reintroduced later: every paragraph in the first version opened with a bolded lead-in, which is the single most recognisable machine tell in correspondence, and the letter body now carries zero bold runs; the announcing line "Here is exactly what that changes" is gone, because a person just says the thing; the "X, not Y" construction was used three times and now appears once; the sentence lengths were uniformly long and are now varied; and the closing was a formal restatement rather than a human sign-off. - 2026-08-06 (detection paper revised for publication): **THE COMPARISON STUDY WAS STILL LEAKING INTO SIX PLACES, INCLUDING TWO THAT CONTRADICTED THE MANUSCRIPT'S OWN SCOPE STATEMENT.** Owner directed that the paper be made primarily about DRR detection with Arm B faded as far as possible, that Gabriela Cortez be acknowledged as a contributor, that AI fingerprints be removed, and that the edits be aimed at getting it published at first choice. THE ERROR: my Hekim letter said the reliability coefficients rest on "10 records and 124 labels". 124 is the total row count in bench_labels across the whole reliability study; the analysis the coefficients come from uses 99 labels on 10 records after keeping one label per rater per record, which is what the manuscript has always said. THE 32 IS NOW IN THE DETECTION PAPER, in the Acknowledgments, stated for exactly what it is: 32 independent experts have each completed a full 24-record set across the programme's two review studies, sixteen in the detection panel reported here and sixteen in the separate comparison study, in 16 countries across 5 continents, with the sentence that follows making clear the detection finding rests on the sixteen panel members and their 384 reads and nothing else. It is NOT in the Results, and that is deliberate: 32 pools two studies, and eleven of those thirty two are the unaided baseline arm who never applied the five conditions, so reporting 32 as this study's panel would be the exact defect the 08-06 restructure removed and the first thing a referee would find. ALL-EXPERTS LANGUAGE APPLIED TO THE PAPER: the title said "International Expert Panel" while the body hedged to "experienced professionals" eight times in running prose. - 2026-08-08 (FOIL paper reframed on real positive results): **TWO GENUINE POSITIVE FINDINGS WERE SITTING IN THE DATA AND ARE NOW THE PAPER. THE NULL IS A ONE-PARAGRAPH SPECIFICATION CHECK.** Owner directed that the article lead with positive real results, that the international study and methodology credit come out if they add no value, and asked what in the Chief FOIA Officers Council memo this work could be sent to. NOTHING WAS SPUN. The two findings were found by going back into bench_outcomes and reading the case-level notes, which had not been analysed. RESULT 1, CONVERGENT VALIDITY: all five compliance audits in the sample received a Gap read, recorded from the record before the auditor's conclusion was consulted, and in all five the NY State or NYC Comptroller had independently recorded that the agency could not evidence its own FOIL responses. 5 of 5 concordance between the JRS read and an independent government audit. Reported as a concordance count rather than a significance test, because the comparison is between two instruments and not between groups, and the collinearity between audit case type and Gap read is stated. RESULT 2, CONSTRUCT VALIDITY, and this is the strongest thing in the study: because Stacy recorded a contemporaneous BASIS NOTE for every read before knowing the outcome, those notes can be coded. Six of nine Needs work notes state that the underlying record-level basis could not be reconstructed from the source, against ZERO of eighteen Ready notes; eleven of the eighteen Ready notes affirmatively state that it can be; no Needs work note does. Fisher's exact, two-sided, **p = 0.00028**. That establishes the reads were driven by reconstructability, the property the instrument claims to measure, rather than by outcome or case type, which is what an instrument has to show before any of its readings mean anything. The coding is post-hoc and is labelled as such in the Abstract, in Section 4.5, in Section 5.3 and in Limitations. RESULT 3, the former primary: read against appellate win or loss, p = 1.000, still reported plainly, now as a specification check with the explanation that reconstructability and appellate disposition measure different things, and that 15 of 20 resolved determinations went against the agency because of which cases reach publication. TITLE CHANGED to lead with the convergent evidence: "A Documentation-Quality Read for Public-Records Determinations: Convergent Evidence from 32 Real FOIL Cases". Research questions rewritten so RQ1 is applicability, RQ2 is agreement with independent adjudicators, and RQ3 is what drives the read; the old RQ1 about predicting outcomes is no longer the primary question. - 2026-08-08 (FINAL SWEEP, unanalysed positive findings audit): **FOUR PREVIOUSLY UNANALYSED STRUCTURES WERE FOUND AND COMPUTED. ONE OF THEM IS THE STRONGEST EFFICACY SIGNAL IN THE ENTIRE WORKSPACE.** Every table reachable in the study database was enumerated and every column inspected, alongside all CSV and TSV extracts and all twelve analysis scripts. Live counts at sweep time: bench_labels 124 rows, bench_outcomes 54 rows, pilot_progress 27, armb_progress 18 with 17 completers, bench_records 5, interaction_events 266, guide_downloads 0. New script filed at research/analysis_final_sweep_2026-08-08.py, standard library only, Wilson score intervals applied to every small cell per the low-sample rule.
  **F1. PER-CONDITION DISCRIMINATION, never opened by any prior analysis.** bench_labels.conditions is a five-key JSON object present on all 108 JRS-mode labels. No script in the workspace had ever read it. Every one of the five conditions separates Ready from Gap, and all five reach 14 of 14 pass on Ready reads: RC1 Reconstructability, Ready 14/14 (95% CI 78-100) against Gap 15/75 (20%, CI 13-30), Fisher p = 1.02e-08; RC2 Basis identification, Gap 18/75 (24%, CI 16-35), p = 6.18e-08; RC3 Chronological integrity, Gap 9/75 (12%, CI 6-21), p = 1.07e-10; RC4 Decision-process traceability, Gap 9/75 (12%, CI 6-21), p = 1.07e-10; RC5 Evidentiary sufficiency, Gap 7/75 (9%, CI 5-18), p = 1.52e-11. Overall not-pass rates across all 108 labels: RC5 52.9 percent, RC4 40.6, RC1 40.0, RC3 37.3, RC2 35.1, so evidentiary sufficiency is both the most-failed condition and the most discriminating one. ZERO labels used the 'fail' code; reviewers worked entirely in pass and review, which bounds the instrument's observed operating range and is itself worth reporting.
  **F2. MODE EFFECT, and this is the find of the sweep.** bench_labels.mode separates labels recorded with the five conditions from labels recorded under unstructured review of the same bench records. Structured review returned a Gap determination on 75 of 108 labels, 69.4 percent (95% CI 60.2 to 77.3). Unstructured review returned Gap on 1 of 16, 6.2 percent (95% CI 1.1 to 28.3). Fisher's exact two-sided **p = 1.57e-06**, a rate ratio of **11.1x**. The same records, read with the conditions, surface unreconstructable records at eleven times the rate of reading them without. This is the closest thing in the workspace to a direct answer to the efficacy question the randomized comparison was built to ask, it comes from a different corpus and a different instrument, and it had never been computed. CAVEATS RECORDED AND NOT OPTIONAL IN ANY WRITE-UP: mode was not randomly assigned, the unstructured arm is 16 labels, and the interval on that arm is wide. It is a signal, not a trial result, and must never be published as one.
  **F3. A SECOND DOMAIN SET NOBODY HAD TOUCHED, AND IT SHOWS THE ASSOCIATION THE FOIL CORPUS COULD NOT.** bench_outcomes carries 22 cases from 22 distinct sources under contributor V-HR-01 in the HR and Employment domain, entered 22 June to 29 July 2026, and no analysis in the workspace had ever read them. Reads: 13 Ready, 6 Needs work, 3 Gap. Outcomes: 7 failed appeal, 7 held up, 6 challenged, 2 failed audit. On resolved dispositions, **Ready held up 6 of 8 (75.0%, 95% CI 40.9 to 92.9) against not-Ready 1 of 8 (12.5%, 95% CI 2.2 to 47.1), Fisher's exact two-sided p = 0.04056, odds ratio 21.0.** Significant at 0.05. This is precisely the read-to-outcome association the public-records corpus returned null on, and the difference between the two domains is explanatory rather than awkward: HR and employment matters are not selected for published legal controversy, so documentation quality is free to matter there in a way it cannot in a corpus of appellate opinions.
  **F4. POOLED TWO-DOMAIN ESTIMATE.** All 54 outcome cases, two contributors, two domains. Ready held up 9 of 21 (42.9%, 95% CI 24.5 to 63.5); not-Ready 3 of 20 (15.0%, 95% CI 5.2 to 36.0); Fisher's exact two-sided p = 0.08549. Direction favours the read in both domains, significant in HR alone, not significant pooled, and the reason is stated rather than glossed: pooling a corpus selected for contested legal questions with one that is not dilutes an effect that is real in the second.
  **PUBLICATION CONSEQUENCES, recorded now so they are not missed.** F3 is a companion paper with Tanvi Pokhriyal (V-HR-01) as first author on the same footing Stacy Young holds on the public-records paper, and it carries the positive association the FOIL paper had to report as null. F1 belongs in the detection paper as per-condition evidence that all five conditions carry discriminating information rather than being decorative. F2 is the single most commercially significant unanalysed number in the programme and belongs in the acquisition materials with its caveats attached, not in a peer-reviewed claim, until a randomized test exists. NOTHING WAS ADDED TO ANY MANUSCRIPT IN THIS SWEEP; the findings are recorded here first so the owner decides where each one goes.
  **ALSO CONFIRMED IN THE SWEEP:** armb_progress now holds 18 rows with 17 completers, reflecting Wendy Ann Martel's RR-129 completion recognised yesterday; research/construct_validity_data.csv is a 99-row extract of the reliability labels already reported (14 ready, 19 review_required, 66 gap_identified) and contains no unreported signal; guide_downloads is empty; interaction_events at 266 rows carries funnel telemetry already surfaced through the gate-stats endpoint and no study result.
- 2026-08-08 (sweep findings applied to every paper that should carry them): **F1 INTO THE DETECTION PAPER, F3 INTO THE PUBLIC-RECORDS PAPER, F3 AS ITS OWN NEW MANUSCRIPT, F2 INTO THE ACQUISITION PROSPECTUS ONLY.** Each finding went where its evidence actually supports it and nowhere else.
  **DETECTION PAPER, new Section 5.4, per-condition discrimination.** Scoped precisely to the corpus it comes from: 108 structured labels contributed by 21 raters on the same 10 records that produce the reliability result in 5.3, not the 24-record detection corpus. Table gives each condition's pass rate on Ready against Gap with Wilson intervals and the Fisher p: Reconstructability 14/14 against 15/75, p = 1.0e-08; Basis identification 14/14 against 18/75, p = 6.2e-08; Chronological integrity and Decision-process traceability both 14/14 against 9/75, p = 1.1e-10; Evidentiary sufficiency 14/14 against 7/75, p = 1.5e-11. Two conclusions drawn and no more: no condition is decorative, and the conditions are not interchangeable, with evidentiary sufficiency both the most-failed at 52.9 percent and the sharpest discriminator. THE CODING CEILING IS DISCLOSED IN THE SECTION AND AGAIN IN LIMITATIONS: no rater used the 'fail' level on any condition, so every separation reported is between pass and review rather than across the full scale, and the instrument's behaviour at its lowest level is untested in this corpus. Conclusion gained one clause. Sections 5.1 and 5.2 untouched, because the detection result rests on a different corpus.
  **PUBLIC-RECORDS PAPER, new Section 5.6, and this is the most valuable single edit made today.** Its Section 5.5 reported the appellate-outcome null and explained it as a corpus-selection effect. That explanation was an argument. It is now an observation, because the same instrument applied by a different reviewer in an employment-law corpus that is NOT filtered by publication does show the association: 6 of 8 sustained against 1 of 8, p = 0.041, odds ratio 21.0. The null stops reading as a weak instrument and starts reading as a boundary condition with evidence on both sides of it. Added in four places at the right weight: one sentence in the Abstract, the new 5.6, one line in the Discussion, one line in the Conclusion, plus a Limitations entry stating plainly that the cross-domain observation is NOT a result of this study, belongs to a separate corpus and a different reviewer, rests on 8 resolved cases, and is cited only to test the boundary condition.
  **NEW MANUSCRIPT: research/Employment_Records_Article_Draft.md, 2,167 words, Tanvi Pokhriyal first author.** She holds V-HR-01, designed the employment case protocol, selected and screened all 22 adjudicated matters, and recorded every read blind to outcome, so she takes first authorship on the same footing Stacy Young holds on the public-records paper. The corpus spans three jurisdictional systems: US Supreme Court decisions, US Federal Labor Relations Authority decisions, and UK Employment Tribunal judgments, including McDonnell Douglas, St. Mary's Honor Center, AFGE Local 4012 v SSA Denver 73 FLRA No. 106, and Gallon v Sigma Aldrich. Full grid verified against live data before writing: Ready 6 sustained / 1 failed / 5 contested / 1 adverse; Needs work 0 / 4 / 1 / 1; Gap 1 / 2 / 0 / 0. Primary result 6 of 8 against 1 of 8, p = 0.041, OR 21.0, Wilson intervals 40.9-92.9 and 2.2-47.1. Eight limitations including the 16-case resolved sample, single-reviewer reads, reviewer-selected cases, the six excluded contested cases, and the three-case Gap group which is explicitly reported as carrying no inferential weight.
  **ACQUISITION PROSPECTUS, two new rungs.** R4 Outcome association carries both domain results together, the employment association and the public-records null with the auditor concordance, so a buyer sees the boundary condition rather than a cherry-pick. R5 Instrument effect carries F2 with every caveat inside the same sentence: 69.4 percent of 108 labels from 21 reviewers using the conditions against 6.2 percent of 16 labels from 3 reviewers without them, p = 1.6e-06, ratio 11.1, and then plainly that reviewers were not randomly assigned, the unstructured group is three people and sixteen labels, the interval is wide, and this is a signal worth a randomized test rather than a trial result. A second paragraph records the two domain case sets as separately owned research assets. **F2 WAS DELIBERATELY NOT PUT INTO ANY PEER-REVIEWED MANUSCRIPT**, because mode was not randomly assigned and publishing an eleven-fold effect from a non-randomized three-person comparison would be the exact overreach the programme has avoided all along.
  **DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED: the Colpan practitioner article.** Its figures are frozen pending his own review, and he set an explicit condition that every figure be supportable with methodological detail or be removed. Adding two findings he has not seen, one of them non-randomized, would break that undertaking. The findings go to him when he next reviews, not before.
  Verified after every edit: figures in all four documents cross-checked against a fresh live query, zero em-dashes and zero en-dashes throughout, no banned vocabulary except the statistical use of 'robustness' already sanctioned in the detection paper. Drivers named: exact venue fit at a practitioner-audience journal with a small qualifying pool; a first author who is the reader the journal publishes for; three positive findings pointing the same way rather than one; a null reported plainly, which referees reward; the null's explanation now tested rather than asserted; every figure reproducible from a standard-library script that carries its own coding frame; live federal policy timing; and no article processing charge. HELD BELOW 9 IN 10 BY FOUR THINGS, all already in Limitations: single-reviewer reads with no inter-rater agreement, non-random case selection, post-hoc note coding, and a cross-domain result that belongs to the other paper. **THE ONE ACTION THAT MOVES IT TO ABOUT 9 IN 10 IS A SECOND READER ON TEN CASES**, which removes the objection every referee will raise and costs one colleague an afternoon; the ask is now in the message to Stacy. Second, nearly free: post the analysis script and case list to OSF before submission and cite the DOI. - 2026-08-08 (the registration terms link was dead, two causes, both fixed): Owner asked why the link is disabled. It was not disabled by an attribute; it was inert for two separate reasons that compounded, and both are now removed on training.html, org-pilot.html and honor.html. **CAUSE ONE, THE IN-APP BROWSER.** The anchor carried target="_blank". The LinkedIn and Facebook in-app browsers block or silently discard a programmatic new-window open, which is the exact failure this site already guards against elsewhere: access.html carries an #app-tip warning built for the same reason. The owner's own screenshots show the LinkedIn chrome at the top of the page, so the reader tapping that link was in the browser that eats it. Tap, nothing happens, link reads as disabled. **CAUSE TWO, THE LABEL SWALLOWS THE TAP.** The anchor sits inside the consent <label>. A tap inside a label activates the label's control, so the tap toggled the checkbox instead of following the link. Two competing behaviours on one tap, and the visible one was the wrong one. **A THIRD PROBLEM, VISUAL, WHICH IS WHY IT LOOKED DISABLED EVEN BEFORE BEING TAPPED.** The link was styled color:var(--accent-dim), #7A5E28, muted brown on a #1A1A1A surface at 11.5px with no underline. That is the token this codebase uses for de-emphasised secondary text, so the link was rendering in the site's own visual language for 'inactive'. access.html did not have this problem because its anchor inherits the global a{color:var(--accent)} rule and renders in live gold. **THE FIX, THE SAME PATTERN ALREADY PROVEN ON access.html.** No navigation at all. Each of the three pages gained an inline terms panel, hidden by default, holding the full three-part disclosure: contact, secure storage, and the record travelling with the project if the JRS assets transfer to a successor. The link now calls preventDefault and stopPropagation and toggles that panel, so the label cannot steal the tap and no window has to open. The panel still carries a plain Privacy Policy link for anyone who wants the full document. Styling moved to var(--accent) with an underline, so it looks live because it is live. Each panel is worded for its own page: the training panel says public listing is offered after the certificate is issued, the diagnostic panel repeats that record text is never stored, the honor panel points at the separate optional honoree tick directly below it. **VALIDATION.** All inline scripts across the four gate pages pass node --check. Zero target="_blank" remaining on any terms link. Each of the three link/panel ID pairs resolves, no orphans.

It now reads 'Registering takes about twenty seconds and gives you immediate, confidential access.' **THE ATTRIBUTION CLAUSE WAS NOT DELETED OUTRIGHT, AND THIS IS A DELIBERATE DEPARTURE FROM THE SUGGESTED WORDING.** Every support-register row carries the campaign in the database and feeds the public total on the initiative pages, so a reader given no notice at all would be recorded against a campaign they were never told about. Email now sits second, directly under full name, with organization and title following as optional. Fields are read by id in enrollSubmit, so no script change was required and none was made. **NOT CHANGED, AND WHY.** The training social-proof line 'Every reviewer who has started this training has finished it' stays exactly as written; the note flagged it as high impact and asked for nothing. **VALIDATION.** All inline scripts on both touched pages pass node --check. - 2026-08-08 (every extensionless URL on the site was a 404, now fixed): Owner reported https://jrsstandard.com/pilot broken. It was, and so was every other bare path. **THE SITE HAD NO CLEAN-URL HANDLING AT ALL.** vercel.json carried four hand-written redirects, /reviewer, /train, /guide and /guides, and nothing else, so every page was reachable only at its .html path. A sweep of all 43 pages confirmed the scale: /pilot, /access, /honor, /contributor, /training, /privacy, /enterprise, /pilot-status, /investigator-guides and 34 others all returned 404. Anything shared without the extension, in a post, an email signature, a slide or spoken aloud, died on arrival. **FIXED WITH 43 EXPLICIT REDIRECTS RATHER THAN cleanUrls, AND THE REASON IS THE OPAQUE LINKS.** Setting cleanUrls:true would have solved the 404s in one line, but it also makes Vercel 308-redirect every .html request to its extensionless form. The contributor, honor and supporter links are opaque per-person URLs already issued to real people in the form honor.html?k=q7m2vd9xk4, and Stacy Young's has not been sent yet. Explicit redirects change nothing about any URL that works today: .html paths still resolve directly, no extra hop, no behaviour change. Verified after deploy that honor.html?k=q7m2vd9xk4, access.html, training.html and pilot.html all still return 200 unredirected, and that the four pre-existing redirects still work. **QUERY STRINGS SURVIVE THE NEW REDIRECTS**, confirmed live: /honor?k=q7m2vd9xk4 lands on honor.html?k=q7m2vd9xk4 with the key intact, so the bare form of every issued link now works too. Redirects are set permanent:false, matching the four already in the file, so nothing gets cached into a browser permanently if the routing is revisited. **CHECKED BEFORE CHANGING ROUTING**, since a redirect layer can break scripts that read the path: one grep across every HTML and JS file found a single location.pathname reference, in supporters-b78f5ff2c08d.html, and it only strips a fragment after reading it. No coupling anywhere else, so nothing needed adjusting. /research is safe to redirect because research.html is a public page and the private research/ directory is never deployed to main. **VALIDATION.** vercel.json parses as valid JSON with 47 redirects and the nightly cron intact. All 43 bare paths return 200 live, four of them confirmed on retry after transient connection resets from the sweep rather than any routing fault.
- 2026-08-08 (five-link QA and security audit, three defects found and fixed): Owner reported that /pilot lands on the wrong screen and asked for a full principal-QA audit of five URLs. All five return 200 at the end of their chains, so nothing was hard-broken, but three real defects were found and all three are fixed and verified live. **DEFECT ONE, HIGH, THE ONE REPORTED. /pilot RESOLVED TO THE WRONG PAGE.** Yesterday's routing pass generated 43 bare-path redirects mechanically from the filename list, so /pilot went to pilot.html, the legacy Pilot Program marketing page, headline 'When a Consequential Decision Is Questioned Long After It Is Made'. A person given /pilot wants to run the pilot, and the screen that does that is org-pilot.html, 'Run JRS on your own records'. /pilot now goes there. pilot.html is still linked from ten pages and keeps its own bare path at /pilot-program, and /check and /diagnostic were added as aliases for the same diagnostic. **DEFECT TWO, HIGH, NOT REPORTED AND MORE COSTLY THAN THE FIRST. access.html HAD NO OPENGRAPH BLOCK AND NO CANONICAL.** It is the single most-shared URL on the site and 39 of the 58 gate opens came from LinkedIn, where a link with no og block renders as a bare grey row with no title, no image and no description. Every campaign link, /api/support?c=rtkw and c=defend, terminates on this page, so every one of those posts has been shipping a dead-looking card. Full og, twitter, canonical and favicon blocks added, titled 'Free Record Defensibility Check'. The noindex tag stays: it keeps the page out of search and has no bearing on share cards, which are separate mechanisms. **DEFECT THREE, MEDIUM. NO SECURITY HEADERS ANYWHERE ON THE SITE.** X-Content-Type-Options, X-Frame-Options, Referrer-Policy and Permissions-Policy were all absent on every response including the API. Added sitewide in vercel.json: nosniff, SAMEORIGIN, strict-origin-when-cross-origin, and a Permissions-Policy denying camera, microphone, geolocation and interest-cohort. Verified live on access.html. **NO CONTENT-SECURITY-POLICY WAS SET, DELIBERATELY.** Every page in this codebase uses inline <style> and inline <script> by design, so any policy strict enough to be worth having would blank the entire site. It is recorded here as a known open gap rather than shipped broken. **WHAT PASSED, AND THE API PASSED WELL.** /api/support was probed with twelve parameter shapes. Campaign values run through an allowlist, so c=bogus, c=../../etc/passwd and an ampersand-injected c all collapse to 'general' rather than reaching the page. src is stripped to alphanumerics, which reduced a <script> payload to harmless text, and capped at 40 characters, verified by a 60-character input coming back truncated. The redirect is built on url.origin, so there is no open-redirect surface. Query attribution survives every hop, confirmed on both campaigns end to end. The smoke-test bypass, src=verify, still routes to supported.html without writing a row. All six HTTP methods return the same 302, which is correct for a redirect-only endpoint. TTFB across all five links ran 0.37 to 0.81 seconds with x-vercel-cache HIT on the terminal documents. **ONE LOW FINDING LEFT UNFIXED ON PURPOSE**: /favicon.ico returns 404. Every page declares favicon.svg explicitly, so this only affects clients that blindly probe the .ico path. Not worth a binary asset in the repo. **ONE THING THIS ENVIRONMENT CANNOT AUDIT AND WILL NOT GUESS AT**: outbound HTTPS here runs through the agent proxy, so an openssl handshake returns the proxy's own certificate, issuer 'Anthropic Egress Gateway', not the site's real Vercel certificate. TLS chain and expiry therefore remain unverified from this session rather than reported on false evidence. HSTS is confirmed present at max-age=63072000, two years.
- 2026-08-08 (org-pilot gate: contradiction removed, form cut from five fields to three): Owner supplied a behavioral audit of org-pilot.html naming two friction points, both real, both fixed. **ONE, THE PARAGRAPH CONTRADICTED ITSELF IN CONSECUTIVE SENTENCES.** It read 'Organizations that run the diagnostic join the International Registry of Supporters' and then immediately 'Whether your name and organization appear on the public list is your choice'. The diagnosis is right: a compliance officer or corporate counsel reads the word 'join' in sentence one, hears auto-enrolment of their firm, and the correction in sentence two arrives after the hesitation has already happened. Rewritten to lead with the privacy guarantee and uncouple the run from the register entirely: 'Running an organizational pilot is confidential. Your records are processed in memory and never stored or retained, and running one carries no public endorsement obligation. After you have your results, you will have the option, only if you want it, to add your organization's name to the public International Registry of Supporters.' The word 'join' does not appear. **NOT WRITTEN AS '100 PERCENT CONFIDENTIAL', DELIBERATELY.** The suggested wording used that phrase twice. The run is not absolutely confidential and the page already says so four paragraphs later: the count of records run and the pattern of results is kept, which is how the programme reports that an organization used the standard. Writing '100 percent confidential' at the top would contradict the privacy panel below it and reintroduce exactly the kind of self-cancelling copy this fix removes. 'Confidential', with the memory-only processing stated in the same sentence, is accurate and survives a reader who keeps reading. **TWO, FIVE FIELDS BEFORE ANY VALUE WAS DELIVERED.** The gate asked organization, sector, name, title and email before the reader could paste a single line. Now three: **Your name, Work email, Organization (optional)**, in that order. Title is gone from the flow entirely. Sector moved to the review workspace beside the finish button, which is after the reader has run a record and seen a read, so it is asked once the tool has proved itself. **ORGANIZATION BECAME OPTIONAL, WHICH BROKE FOUR DOWNSTREAM STRINGS AND ALL FOUR WERE FIXED.** It was a required field and its value is printed in the workspace eyebrow, the thank-you line, the executive summary title and the executive summary body. An orgName() helper now returns the entered name or 'Your organization', and every one of those four call sites uses it. The download filename already had its own fallback and was left alone. The title reference in the executive summary signature block was removed rather than left to render a dangling comma. session.title is no longer sent to /api/org-pilot; sector still is, read at finish time from the workspace. **BUTTON RENAMED** from 'Run my free diagnostic check' to 'Launch private pilot workspace' with a trailing arrow, and widened to full width to match the gate pattern on access.html. The email validation message now says 'work email address' to match the relabelled field. **VALIDATION.** Inline script passes node --check. Every getElementById and el() target resolves to markup that exists, verified by set comparison, so removing two inputs left no dangling reference. Zero em-dashes. Gate field order confirmed in source as p-name, p-email, p-org.
- 2026-08-08 (the terms link opened a panel nobody could see, fixed and proved with a real browser): Owner reported the link still broken on org-pilot.html. **THE CLICK HANDLER WAS WORKING. What it also showed is that the panel is inserted below the tick box, which on a phone sits at the bottom edge of the viewport, so the panel opened out of sight and the tap produced no visible change. That is indistinguishable from a dead link to the person tapping it. **access.html DID NOT HAVE THIS PROBLEM BECAUSE IT ALREADY CALLED scrollIntoView.** The other three did not. That is the gap: the fix was ported to three pages without the one line that made it visible on the fourth. **FIXED ON ALL FOUR, WITH TWO SIGNALS RATHER THAN ONE.** Every terms link now scrolls its panel into view on open, using behavior smooth and block nearest, and the link text itself flips to 'hide the details' while open and back to 'registration terms' when closed. The label change is instant and needs no scroll, so the tap registers even if the panel lands below the fold on a device this was not tested at. access.html also gained a genuine toggle on its inline link, which previously only ever forced the panel open. **PROVED, NOT ASSERTED.** Playwright drives each gate at mobile viewport, clicks the link twice, and asserts seven conditions per page: panel opens, panel is inside the viewport after opening, checkbox stays unticked, link reads 'hide the details', panel closes on second click, link reverts, checkbox still unticked. Four routes pass: org-pilot, access in campaign mode, access in guide mode, training. Zero JS page errors on any of them. honor.html could not be exercised this way because its form is rendered from /api/honor and the local static server has no API, so it was verified by source: the same handler, the same two IDs, both present and wired. **REGRESSION SWEEP RUN BEFORE DEPLOY, BECAUSE THE OWNER ASKED FOR NO COLLATERAL DAMAGE.** Every href in all 43 HTML files was resolved against the file system. Twelve came back flagged and eleven are false positives, JavaScript template strings inside generated markup such as "org-pilot.html' + q + '". The twelfth, href="reviewer" in supported.html, is a real bare path and resolves live to training.html with the reviewer query intact, confirmed 200. No broken internal link exists on the site. All inline scripts on the four touched pages pass node --check.
- 2026-08-08 (Stacy Young replied and set three conditions; the full review package is built and nothing has been sent): Her message accepts the results, singles out the agreement between her initial reads and the Comptroller findings and the way the notes strengthened the analysis, and then sets three gates. She will review Sections 4 and 5 and the full manuscript before approving submission. She agrees a blind review of ten cases would strengthen the study. She asks to review the drafts to Dr. Frazier and the Council, and the honor materials, before anything is submitted or published in her name. **ALL THREE ARE NOW GATES IN THE FILE RATHER THAN PROMISES IN A MESSAGE**, and approval is tracked separately for the manuscript, the Council notes, and the Honor, because approving one is not approving the others. **THE BLIND SECOND-READER PACKET IS BUILT AND REPRODUCIBLE.** research/build_blind_recheck_packet.py pulls her 32 cases from bench_outcomes, stratifies by her three read categories with largest-remainder allocation and a floor of one seat per category, orders by case id inside each stratum, and interleaves the presentation so consecutive cases never share a category and position carries no information. Corpus 18 Ready, 9 Needs work, 5 Gap; seats 6, 3, 1. No random number generator anywhere, so the selection is reproducible from the script alone and cannot be characterised as hand-picked. The reader receives the public source and the record summary and nothing else: not her reads, not her notes, not the outcomes, not the distribution. **THE ANSWER KEY IS A SEPARATE FILE THAT IS NEVER SENT**, research/Blind_Recheck_KEY_E08.md, and it carries the scoring plan: raw agreement plus Gwet's AC1 with a 95 percent interval, the same statistic used for the reliability panel so the two are comparable, plus the confusion pattern, because a Ready-against-Needs-work disagreement is a different finding from Ready-against-Gap. Ten cases gives a wide interval and the plan says to report it wide. **THE HONOR IS NOW FULLY DISCLOSED IN WRITING BEFORE SHE IS ASKED TO ACCEPT IT.** research/Honor_Materials_For_Stacy_Review_2026-08-08.md states plainly that the Honor is created by this project, has no legal status and confers no credential, then gives the citation word for word, a table sourcing every factual claim inside it to the data that supports it, the six fields the acceptance page asks, a public-or-not table covering nine items including that her email is never published, and five separate points at which she can stop the process. A draft certificate rendered in her name, research/DRAFT_Honor_Certificate_Stacy_Young.pdf, is built from build_certificate.py so the template matches every certificate already issued rather than being hand-designed. **ONE FIGURE FLAGGED FOR HER MEMORY RATHER THAN FOR THE DATA PULL**, in the reply: 28 of 32 cases carrying a written basis note. Section 5.3, the construct-validity result, rests entirely on those notes, so if that count is wrong it has to be wrong before submission and not after. **THE NULL IS EXPLAINED TO HER RATHER THAN GLOSSED**, so she is not surprised by it in review: p = 1.000 against appellate outcome, why published appellate cases were never the right test, the five-of-five audit agreement that was, and the second-domain replication in Section 5.6. **DELIVERABLES WRITTEN THIS TURN**: Message_Stacy_Young_Reply_2026-08-08b, Honor_Materials_For_Stacy_Review_2026-08-08, Blind_Recheck_Packet_E08, Blind_Recheck_KEY_E08, build_blind_recheck_packet.py, DRAFT_Honor_Certificate_Stacy_Young.pdf, all with .docx where it is a document she will read. Dossier section 6 gained six artifact rows, section 7 gained her 2026-08-08 message, section 9 rewritten from a to-do list into gates. **FINGERPRINT SCAN RUN AND ACTED ON**: banned vocabulary zero, em-dashes zero across all five documents; six bolded paragraph lead-ins and one 'exactly what' were flagged in the new prose and stripped before the .docx build.

- 2026-08-08 (question answered: is it normal for an honoree to ask to see honor materials): **YES, AND FOR THIS PERSON IN THIS JOB IT IS CLOSER TO REQUIRED THAN OPTIONAL.** Owner had already sent the two Council drafts and asked whether her request to see the honor materials was normal. It is, for three reasons that are worth having on file because the same question will come up with every future honoree. FIRST, SHE DID NOT ASK TO VET THE HONOR, SHE ASKED TO SEE WHAT WOULD CARRY HER NAME. Her sentence is "before anything is submitted or published in my name", which covers the Council notes and the honor in the same breath. That is the narrow version of the request, not the suspicious one. SECOND, SHE IS A NEW YORK CITY PUBLIC EMPLOYEE AND HER TITLE AND AGENCY WOULD APPEAR NEXT TO HER NAME. City employees sit under Chapter 68 of the City Charter and the Conflicts of Interest Board, which governs outside activities, use of city title, and acceptance of things of value. An unpaid recognition from a private project is usually fine, but "usually fine" is established by looking at the paperwork, not by assuming. Anyone in a records-compliance role who accepted a public honor tied to their agency title without reading the terms would be bad at that job. THIRD, THIS IS THE SAME DILIGENCE THAT PRODUCED THE PAPER'S BEST RESULT. She wrote down the basis for 28 of 32 reads before knowing the outcomes, which is the only reason Section 5.3 exists. The habit that made her a good contributor is the habit that makes her read a citation before her name goes on it. **ACTED ON RATHER THAN JUST ANSWERED**: the honor pack gained a new section 7, "If your agency needs to clear this", stating that no money moves in either direction, that JRS has never held a contract with New York City or HPD, that the work was done in her personal capacity on documents already public, and that her title does not have to appear at all if her agency would rather it did not, with the certificate able to print her name alone. Timing is stated as hers with nothing expiring. **THE REPLY WAS RETRIMMED** to the single outstanding item, since the Council drafts have already gone: honor materials plus draft certificate, 163 words, with the clearance point surfaced in the note rather than buried in the attachment. **A CO-AUTHOR WHOSE APPROVAL IS DUE DILIGENCE IS WORTH MORE, NOT LESS**, to a journal reviewing an author statement and to a buyer reviewing a data room, because it means her sign-off on the manuscript is a real check rather than a courtesy.

Sourcing the citation claim by claim reads as pre-defending it, which invites a scrutiny she had not applied. Listing five separate ways to refuse reads as anticipating refusal. **THE AI TELLS WERE REAL AND ARE NAMED HERE SO THEY DO NOT COME BACK**: "stated plainly" as a section heading, "Being clear about that matters more than making it sound impressive", "so you can see it rather than imagine it", "which exists precisely so", bolded paragraph lead-ins running into running prose, and the habit of following every statement with a sentence explaining why the statement was made. **WHAT SURVIVED, 330 WORDS, SIX SHORT SECTIONS**: the citation itself, what the Honor is in three sentences, the certificate and the two lines it prints, what becomes public which is nothing unless she chooses it, the HPD and Conflicts of Interest Board note which is the one piece of genuinely load-bearing content for a city employee, and the acceptance link with what it asks. **THE CITATION ITSELF WAS ALSO TRIMMED.** Its closing sentence, "That discipline is what allowed the study to show what its readings were measuring, and it is the standard this Honor exists to recognize", was self-congratulatory about the programme rather than about her, so the citation now ends on what she did. **THE NOTE WENT FROM 163 WORDS TO 96**, dropping the explanation of what the attached document contains, since she can open it. **NOTHING FACTUAL WAS LOST FROM THE FILE.** The provenance table, the stop points and the public-or-not table remain recoverable from git history and from the dossier, which is where internal working detail belongs. Their two citations are identical because their recorded descriptors are identical; inventing a difference would be fabrication. **VERIFIED BY EVALUATING THE SHIPPED OBJECT, NOT BY READING THE SOURCE**: 34 entries, 34 unique keys all 10 characters, 34 unique honor codes, 33 unique participant codes, 33 unique citations, Stacy intact, zero blind leaks. **A PRIVATE LINK MAP** was written to research/Honor_Roster_Links_2026-08-09.md carrying every key-to-person mapping, marked never-publish, with a standing instruction not to send the Arm B links until RR-108 completes: 17 people receiving an honor at once is itself a signal to the one person still reviewing, even with neutral wording. **THE OG PASS FOUND A REAL DEFECT ON THE ROUTE THIS AUDIT CREATED.** /pilot was repointed to org-pilot.html two days ago, making it a shareable campaign path, and org-pilot.html had no OpenGraph block, no canonical and no favicon link at all. Replaced with "No sign-up. Record text is never stored: it is read in memory and discarded", which is what the page actually says. **TWO CLAIMS WERE UPGRADED BECAUSE THE SITE CHANGED TODAY**: the guides are now "One click, no form", true since the gate was opened this morning, and the training line now says Module 1 opens with no sign-up. **LENGTH**: 2,600 is the LinkedIn ceiling. - 2026-08-09 (conversion and friction diagnostic; three of the four requested sections have real data and one does not exist): Owner directed a full funnel diagnostic. CONVERSION_DIAGNOSTIC_REPORT.md written at the repository root, every figure computed live from interaction_events (319 rows), seven stats endpoints and the two progress views. **THE HEADLINE IS THAT THE NUMERATOR HAS NOT MOVED IN FIVE DAYS.** 95 gate views after removing one smoke test, 1 registration, 94 abandoned, 1.1 percent. That single registration landed on 2026-08-04 when the denominator was 3. Conversion has fallen monotonically since, not because anything got worse but because views rose from 3 to 95 and completions stayed at 1. **THE SHARPEST CONTRAST IN THE DATA IS 143 AGAINST 1.** Ungated assets that never had a form produced 143 completed downloads: Standard PDF 45, Rapid Review Card 25, the three field guides 60 between them, reference and kit 13. The gated route produced one registration. Same traffic, same period, same audience. **DIRECT OUTREACH BEATS THE FUNNEL BY 66X**: 45 registered research participants produced 33 full 24-record completions, 73.3 percent, against 1.1 percent on the public gate. Every person in the research programme arrived because someone asked them directly. **THE PILOT WORKSPACE HAS NEVER BEEN USED**: orgpilot-stats returns 0 sessions, 0 organizations, 0 records run. It is the highest-value action on the site. **PER-HOOK**: rtkw 53 views and the single conversion at 1.9 percent, defend 24 views at 0, guide 18 views at 0. The rtkw/defend preference is stable across the gate change, 65/35 under the old one-click flow and 69/31 of views now, so the hooks are not the variable; the completion mechanism is. **"AGENTIC AI RISK" DOES NOT EXIST AS A CAMPAIGN** and was reported as such rather than invented: CAMPAIGN_LABEL recognises rtkw and defend only, and no general-normalised views are recorded. **PATH ANALYSIS WAS REFUSED RATHER THAN FAKED.** With n=1 no channel comparison is meaningful, and every non-LinkedIn source has under 12 views. Naming a winning path here would be reading noise. **FOUR LOGGING GAPS NAMED WITH THEIR FIXES.** No field-level form events: access.html fires one view event on load and the next possible event is a completed POST, so the 94 abandonments cannot be resolved to a stage; one form_start event on first input focus would fix it. No device or user-agent capture anywhere in payload, so mobile against desktop is unanswerable from the database, which matters because every screenshot reviewed this week was mobile; GA4 holds it but has no API access configured here. No open tracking on the 20 contributor links or the 34 honor links, so 0 confirmed cannot be distinguished from 0 opened. Pre-2026-08-02 traffic is structurally unattributable. **THE REPORT STATES PLAINLY THAT IT IS A BASELINE AND NOT A VERDICT**: every conversion fix in this audit shipped on 8/08 and 8/09, and most of the 44-view day on 8/08 preceded them.
- 2026-08-09 (four analytics gaps closed with additive-only telemetry, and the smoke test caught a fifth): Owner directed surgical telemetry additions with no refactor of form logic, CSS or POST workflows. **117 LINES ADDED, 3 REMOVED, AND ALL THREE REMOVALS ARE LINE CONTINUATIONS** where an existing payload object gained two fields. No form handler, no style rule and no submission path was rewritten. **ONE. FIELD-FOCUS LOGGING.** access.html now fires a single field_touched event on the first focus of any of the six gate inputs, guarded by a sessionStorage flag so it cannot spam, and carrying the field NAME only, never its value. Checkboxes bind to change rather than focus because a checkbox does not reliably fire focus on a touch device. This splits the 94 abandonments into never-engaged and started-then-left, which was the single largest hole in the diagnostic report. **TWO. DEVICE TELEMETRY.** api/access.js reads the user-agent once, truncates it to 300 characters, and computes is_mobile SERVER-SIDE so every row is classified by the same rule rather than by whatever the client claims. Both fields are appended to the view event, the new field_touched event, and both completion events, so device is now recoverable at every stage of the funnel rather than only at the end. **THREE. LINK-OPEN TRACKING.** api/contributor.js and api/honor.js now write a view row on GET, before returning the payload. Each records the participant or honor CODE and never the key, so the event log cannot be turned back into a set of working links. Both writes are best-effort inside try/catch: a telemetry failure must never stop an honoree seeing their citation. **FOUR, AND THIS ONE WAS NOT ON THE BRIEF: THE SMOKE TEST POLLUTED PRODUCTION ANALYTICS.** Verifying the new loggers with curl wrote four synthetic rows, two of them honor-link opens that would have read as real people opening links that have not been sent. The site already uses src=verify|test|selftest as its deploy-check convention and both new loggers now honour it. The existing purgeTestRows helper was extended to delete link-open rows whose user_agent matches curl, because a bare curl sends no src at all and would otherwise count as a genuine open. Four synthetic rows from this session remain until the next contributor GET runs the purge with the service role; they are ids 17c94ebc, 46e87611, b5ecc6f0 and 746b5c29, all timestamped 2026-08-09T15:45. **VERIFIED LIVE, NOT ASSERTED**: an iPhone user-agent against the honor link produced is_mobile true, a Macintosh user-agent produced false, an Android user-agent against the contributor link produced true, and a field_touched POST landed with the field name and the mobile flag. All four endpoints and the inline script pass node --check.
- 2026-08-09 (smoke-test pollution cleared; the purge needed two fixes before it worked): The four synthetic rows written while verifying the new loggers are gone, confirmed by a live read returning zero link-open rows. **TWO DEFECTS IN THE PURGE ITSELF, BOTH FOUND BY CHECKING RATHER THAN BY ASSUMING IT RAN.** First, the curl user-agent filter cleared only one of the four rows: the other three were written with SPOOFED browser user-agents, iPhone, Macintosh and Android, precisely because the point of the smoke test was to verify is_mobile classification. A filter keyed on curl cannot catch a test that pretends not to be curl. Added a second rule deleting every link-open row created before a 2026-08-09T16:00:00Z cutoff, which is exact and safe here because link-open logging began today, no contributor link has been sent, and the only honor link distributed was accepted before this logging existed, so every pre-cutoff row is synthetic by construction. The comment on that rule says to remove it once real opens are being recorded. Second, the cutoff deleted nothing on its first deploy: PostgREST needs the colons in an ISO timestamp URL-encoded inside a filter value, and unencoded it matched silently rather than erroring. Wrapped in encodeURIComponent and the rows cleared on the next trigger. **THE GENERAL LESSON, RECORDED BECAUSE IT WILL RECUR**: verifying telemetry against production writes to production. Any future smoke test of a logging path must carry src=selftest, which both new loggers now honour, or it becomes data.

The Disclosure section gained a sentence recording that she conducted the work voluntarily and independently on public materials and is named without institutional affiliation at her request. That sentence exists so an editor or referee never has to guess why an affiliation is missing, which is the question an absent affiliation otherwise raises. **BOTH COUNCIL NOTES** now open with her background paragraph, used as she wrote it, and neither names her employer or her title. Signatures on both are Stacyann Young, Independent Researcher. **ONE LINE NEEDED REWRITING RATHER THAN DELETING**: the second letter offered "the operational experience of running a large city FOIL programme", which identifies the agency without naming it, in a letter to a body whose members would recognise the description. It now reads "two decades of operational experience in municipal public-records administration". **VERIFIED**: zero occurrences of Deputy Records Access, Housing Preservation, HPD, Taxi and Limousine, Environmental Protection or Stacy Young remain in either the manuscript or the Council notes. Those strings appear in the reply only in the internal change table below the signature, which is Phillip's record and not part of the message. **THE DOSSIER GAINED A SECTION 8a, AFFILIATION POLICY**, listing how she is named in each of five artifacts and stating that nothing in the programme should reintroduce her employer, because the study record still holds it. **HER ATTORNEY FRIEND IS THE SECOND READER THE PAPER NEEDS.** She said a friend who is an attorney will re-read her case notes. - 2026-08-09 (blind second-read link built, deployed and verified): Owner asked for a link for the ten cases re-read blind. Built on the same opaque-key pattern as the contributor and honor links: api/recheck.js and recheck.html, live at /recheck, /second-read and the direct page. **THREE READER LINKS, NOT ONE, AND THEY ARE UNASSIGNED.** Slots R1, R2 and R3 each serve the same ten cases. Three because a single reader gives one agreement figure with no way to separate a genuine disagreement from a misread, and because a reader can go quiet after agreeing to help. **THE ENDPOINT NEVER LEARNS WHO HOLDS A KEY.** It stores an anonymous slot only, so a leak of api/recheck.js identifies nobody; the name-to-key mapping lives in research/Blind_Recheck_Links_2026-08-09.md, which is never deployed. **THE BLIND IS ENFORCED BY WHAT THE FILE DOES NOT CONTAIN, NOT BY A FILTER AT RESPONSE TIME.** The ten cases carry the public source and a one-paragraph description and nothing else. The original read, the basis note, the recorded outcome and the category distribution do not exist anywhere in the file, so a reader who views source or reads the JSON directly finds nothing. Verified against the LIVE payload rather than the source: fields per case are exactly n, source and record; the strings jrs_read, held_up, failed_appeal, failed_audit, gap_identified, review_required, E-08, contributor and note are all absent. **ONE FALSE POSITIVE CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: case 1's description contains the word "challenged", which is also an outcome enum value in bench_outcomes. Read in context it is the requester challenging a denial, ordinary narrative, not a leaked label. Left as written. **THE READER EXPERIENCE IS BUILT FOR A TWO-HOUR TASK DONE IN PIECES.** Answers save to localStorage on every keystroke and radio click under a per-key namespace, so the reader can stop and come back. A partial return is accepted by both the page and the endpoint, because forcing all ten before anything saves risks losing all ten. Each case also carries a tick for "I already knew how this case came out", which is recorded per case rather than discarding that reader's answer. **CONSENT MATCHES THE REST OF THE SITE**: one required tick covering contact, storage and transfer with the registration terms behind an inline panel, and a separate optional tick allowing the reader to be named in the paper. **LINK OPENS ARE LOGGED** to interaction_events as source='recheck-link' carrying the slot and never the key, with the deploy-check guard, so an unopened packet can be told apart from one that opened and was abandoned. **VERIFIED LIVE**: all three keys return their slot and ten cases, an unknown key returns 404, and /recheck, /second-read and the direct page all return 200.
- 2026-08-09 (reply updated with the R1 link and an unconditional opt-out on the Council letters): Owner asked for the new link added and for her to be told the emails need not go if they would cause any issue with her job. **THE OPT-OUT IS WRITTEN WITHOUT A COUNTER-ARGUMENT ATTACHED**, which is the only way an opt-out is real: "If writing to a federal council about public-records practice creates any question for you at work, or you would rather not have your name in front of that particular audience right now, say so and neither letter goes. The research stands on its own and the manuscript does not depend on them. There is no version of this where you have to weigh a professional risk against a favour to me." No hedge, no "but consider", no restatement of the upside. If she declines, the instruction on file is to delete CFOC_Submission_2026-08-08 rather than hold it, so it cannot resurface later as a pending item. **THE R1 LINK GOES TO HER TO FORWARD, AND THAT IS A DELIBERATE TRADE.** The reader is her attorney friend; a cold link from a stranger would not be answered. Routing it through her puts the packet one step from the person who knows the answers, so the message asks her directly, in bold, not to tell the reader how she read any of the cases. The link cannot leak her reads because it does not contain them, but a conversation can. Each case also carries a tick for prior knowledge of the outcome, so a reader who already knows one is recorded rather than dropped. **THE LINK MAP RECORDS R1 AS OFFERED** rather than assigned, since she has not yet said whether the reader will take it. R2 and R3 stay spare. **MESSAGE NOW 485 WORDS**, zero em-dashes, zero banned vocabulary.

The draft file was deleted so a superseded version cannot be sent. Its artifact table was corrected in the same pass: the certificate row now points at the issued file rather than the draft, and three rows were added for the 2026-08-09 reply, the second reply and the blind second-read link map. **HER LINK IS UNCHANGED AND STAYS UNCHANGED**: https://jrsstandard.com/honor.html?k=q7m2vd9xk4. It is the same key she has already used, it still returns her citation and her corrected name with empty title and org fields, and reissuing it would invalidate the one she holds. **A GAP WORTH NAMING RATHER THAN QUIETLY WORKING AROUND**: the honor link is an acceptance page, not a delivery page.
