- 2026-08-09 (two gaps closed for the acquisition record, and a third found while closing them): Owner stated the per-person links exist to collect data for the sale of the IP asset, and directed the gaps fixed. **GAP ONE, CERTIFICATE DELIVERY.** api/honor.js was an acceptance endpoint that could never give an honoree the certificate; every issue was a manual send, tolerable once and 33 times over for the roster. api/honor-cert.js now renders the certificate on demand from the same unguessable key. It uses the name and title the honoree CONFIRMED on acceptance rather than the roster defaults, which is the whole reason acceptance captures them, and falls back to the roster with a visible preview notice if they have not accepted, so the link is never a dead end. Output is a self-contained printable HTML page rather than a PDF because an edge function cannot run the reportlab generator; the layout matches build_certificate.py and every browser can print it to PDF. Verified live on Stacyann Young's key: title Certificate of Recognition, name Stacyann Young, no title line, date 9 August 2026, no preview notice because she has accepted, and Stacy Young, Deputy Records Access, Housing Preservation and HPD all absent. An "Open my certificate" button now sits on the acceptance screen. **GAP TWO, NO DILIGENCE VIEW OF THE LINK PROGRAMMES.** The site had five stats endpoints and none answered the question a buyer asks, which is not page views but how many named professionals engaged and what the funnel from link issued to work completed looks like. api/asset-stats.js rolls up the three per-person programmes and the research participation totals into one live reading. Aggregate counts only: no name, email, organization or key leaves it, because an endpoint that answered the diligence question by exposing a contact list would destroy the consent position that makes the list transferable. Live: 33 full-set completers, 16 detection and 17 comparison, 24 reliability raters, 10 records, corpora of 32 public-records and 22 employment cases, 144 public artifact downloads. **GAP THREE, FOUND WHILE VERIFYING GAP TWO, AND IT WOULD HAVE CORRUPTED THE FIGURE THE WHOLE EXERCISE EXISTS TO PRODUCE.** The first asset-stats read showed 1 honor link opened and 1 recheck link opened. Neither was a third party. The event rows carried iPhone and Windows Chrome user-agents at 17:21 to 17:27, which is the owner opening his own links to check them. A buyer reading "links opened" would have read owner previews as external engagement. All four per-person loggers now honour ?src=owner and ?owner=1 and write nothing, verified by hitting three of them with real browser user-agents and confirming zero rows. The purge was widened from two sources to all four and its cutoff advanced to 2026-08-09T18:00Z, and every preview row was cleared: the table now reads zero. asset-stats carries a link_metrics_basis note stating that opens count from that stamp forward, that previews and deploy checks are suppressed at write time, and that the counts are small because 33 of 34 honor links and all 20 contributor links are deliberately unsent rather than because engagement was measured and found low.
- 2026-08-09 (the /reviewer suite built: landing, evaluation, completion, plus two endpoints): Owner supplied a full spec for a three-page reviewer pathway. Built and live. **TWO DEPARTURES FROM THE SPEC, BOTH DELIBERATE AND BOTH RECORDED IN THE FILES.** First, the supplied CSS was a blue light theme, --accent #2563eb on --bg #f8fafc with system fonts. CLAUDE.md section II locks the palette at #050505 background, #BE9447 accent, Bodoni/JetBrains/Inter. Shipping the supplied CSS would have produced two visually unrelated sites reached through the same nav, which is the exact impression a diligence reader treats as a warning sign. Built in the site palette. Second, the six module names in the spec, Evidentiary Standards, AI Justification Deficits, Applying JRS, Audit Trails and Risk, Practical Diagnostics, Governance Sign-off, are not the six that exist. The live training teaches JRS Review Conditions, Common Documentation Conditions, Secondary Review and Escalation, AI-Assisted Record Review, Investigation Review, Implementation Sequence. A landing page advertising six modules that do not exist would be discovered by the first person who clicked through. Real names used. **THE ROUTE CONFLICT WAS RESOLVED WITHOUT BREAKING ANYTHING.** /reviewer already redirected to training.html, which is a 279KB page carrying six working modules, localStorage progress, simulators, survey and certificate generation, with 7 live enrolments and 4 recorded completions. /reviewer is now the landing page and the training keeps /train and /training and is linked from it, so no enrolment or resume state was touched. **THE EVALUATION IS THE ASSET PIECE.** The programme can already say how consistently the five conditions are applied under study conditions. It cannot say what record review looks like inside working organizations, and that is the field-evidence gap no further accuracy testing fills. Nine questions covering how many people read a record before finalization, whether a formal second reader exists, how often records are returned to the drafter, whether the basis is recorded at the time, whether AI drafting is in use and whether a policy covers it, confidence that a two-year-old record would still explain itself, whether documentation quality has ever been audited, and workflow usefulness on a five-point scale. **ANSWERS AND IDENTITY ARE WRITTEN AS TWO UNLINKED ROWS.** The evaluation goes to interaction_events as an anonymous row carrying only answers, sector, coarse organization size and country. The certificate identity goes to pilot_contacts as a separate row carrying name, email and completion code and NOT the answers. They share nothing that links them. That is what lets the instrument ask a reviewer whether their own employer has no second reader and get an honest answer, and it is why the consent tick for research is separate from the contact tick. Options are validated server-side against the question set, so a hand-crafted POST cannot inject free text into the research record. **LINKEDIN, STATED ACCURATELY RATHER THAN AS SPECIFIED.** The spec asked for "LinkedIn Verification and Skill Endorsement". LinkedIn exposes no interface by which a third party verifies a credential or endorses a skill on someone else's behalf, so neither is claimed. What is built is the real feature: the documented Add to Profile flow, prefilled with certification name, issuing organization, issue month and year, credential ID and credential URL, which creates a Licenses and Certifications entry. Alongside it, a copyable post and a copyable skills list the holder adds themselves, labelled as such. **api/reviewer-cert.js RENDERS THE CERTIFICATE** on the same printable-HTML pattern as honor-cert, checks the completion code for shape only, and states in its own comments why: a certificate grants access to nothing, so protecting it like a credential would add friction without protecting anything, and the verifiable record is the database row. Every render logs to interaction_events. **VERIFIED LIVE**: all seven routes 200, /train and /training unchanged, the instrument returns 9 questions and 11 sectors and 6 sizes and 9 roles, a valid code renders and a malformed one returns 400.
- 2026-08-09 (reviewer suite driven end to end on production; one defect found and fixed during the test): Owner asked whether it is operable, for a message to send with it, for a programme summary, whether participants are documented, and whether it still uses the same free training. **OPERABLE, VERIFIED BY DRIVING IT RATHER THAN BY ASSERTING IT.** Landing page loads with the real module names and the correct CTA target; the instrument returns 9 questions, 11 sectors, 6 sizes and 9 roles; a full nine-answer submission with a certificate request returns answered 9 of 9 and issues a completion code; the certificate renders from that code with the correct name, title and code printed; a malformed code returns 400; all seven routes return 200; /train and /training are unchanged. **THE DEFECT: THE EVALUATION ENDPOINT HAD NO DEPLOY-CHECK GUARD**, so verifying it wrote a fabricated row into the research baseline, which is the one table in this programme that must contain only real answers. Guard added, and it returns a full valid response including a completion code while writing nothing, so the whole path can still be tested. **THE PURGE THEN FAILED TWICE BEFORE IT WORKED, AND BOTH FAILURES WERE THE SAME CLASS OF BUG AS THE TIMESTAMP FILTER EARLIER TODAY.** PostgREST needs the JSON arrow operator URL-encoded inside a filter path: payload->>src matched nothing silently rather than erroring. Encoded as payload-%3E%3Esrc it cleared. The identical latent bug was sitting in the two curl-user-agent filters added earlier and was fixed in the same pass, so the link-open purge that appeared to work was in fact only working through its second rule. Baseline now reads zero reviewer-eval rows. **PROGRAMME SUMMARY WRITTEN** at research/Reviewer_Program_Summary_2026-08-09.md: the five-step pathway, the six real modules, the nine evaluation questions with an explanation of why questions 1, 2, 3 and 8 measure whether the control exists anywhere today while 5 and 6 measure how fast the problem is arriving and 9 is a stated demand signal, the two-unlinked-rows design, a five-row documentation table naming exactly what is stored where and whether it identifies anyone, the routing table showing the training is untouched, the end-to-end verification, and three open items including that the baseline is empty by construction because the instrument shipped today. **MESSAGE WRITTEN** at research/Message_Reviewer_Training_Invite_2026-08-09.md in three versions: cold, already-in-the-programme, and a one-liner. Sending notes attached covering which link to use and why not /train, source tagging so channels are measurable, ?owner=1 for self-checks, an instruction never to describe the certificate as an accreditation because the person most likely to test that claim is the one most worth impressing, and a note that the evaluation is worth sending on its own to anyone who will not do six modules.
- 2026-08-10 (five-phase audit of the /reviewer architecture; two defects found, both in the measurement layer, both fixed): Owner directed a comprehensive end-to-end technical audit with test tags. Every check was driven against live production rather than read from source and asserted, except the three sections that state they are source inspections. REVIEWER_SYSTEM_AUDIT_REPORT.md written at the repository root. **RESULT: 7 of 7 routes pass, 4 of 4 endpoints pass, zero leakage in either direction between the answer row and the contact row, and the research baseline reads 0.** **DEFECT ONE, HIGH, AND THE AUDIT ITSELF CREATED IT BEFORE CATCHING IT.** /api/access had no deploy-check guard. The three device-classification tests wrote live rows into gate telemetry, and gate-view rows are the denominator of the conversion figures in CONVERSION_DIAGNOSTIC_REPORT.md, so a curl test becomes a real visitor who abandoned and the conversion rate a buyer reads is understated by exactly that much. Fixed in three parts: both the view and field_touched writes now honour the standard test tags; the guarded path still returns the computed is_mobile so classification stays testable without writing; and the shared purge clears any test-tagged gate-view row. Verified after: 0 test-tagged rows across 101 gate-view rows. Re-run under the guard returned is_mobile true for iPhone and Android and false for desktop, with recorded:false. **DEFECT TWO, MEDIUM.** The evaluation CTA on the landing page carried no source tag, so a visitor who skipped the training and went straight to the instrument was invisible in the funnel. Now carries src=reviewer-landing. access=reviewer and focus=1 are correctly absent from that link because both are training-page parameters. **INJECTION TEST PASSED CLEANLY.** A payload carrying a script tag, a DROP TABLE string, an out-of-range scale value and fabricated sector, size and role values, alongside one valid answer, returned answered:1. Every injected string was dropped by the server-side option validation and only the valid answer counted. **TABLE ISOLATION VERIFIED FIELD BY FIELD.** The evaluation row carries answers, sector, org size, role, country and modules completed and carries no name, email, organization or printed title. The certificate row carries the identity and the completion code and carries no answers, no answered count and no sector. No field links them beyond a coarse timestamp. **ONE FALSE POSITIVE CHECKED RATHER THAN REPORTED**: a grep for an IP address matched inside the header name x-vercel-ip-country. No IP is stored anywhere; the only network-derived value is the two-letter country code. **CERTIFICATE CODE VALIDATION**: seven shapes tested, valid and minimum-length return 200, and malformed, bad-character, empty, over-length and missing-name all return 400. **LINKEDIN**: all seven Add to Profile parameters present and encoded, and the page states plainly that LinkedIn does not let a third party endorse a skill, so no verification or endorsement is claimed. **LAYOUT INTEGRITY**: 1,145 insertions and 1 deletion across HTML in the window, and the single deletion is the honor.html line replaced by the certificate button. No stylesheet, token or layout rule touched, no production record overwritten.
- 2026-08-10 (FINAL SWEEP executed: exhaustive re-inspection of every raw dataset for unanalysed positive findings; four genuinely new results, one correction, one refusal): Owner directed an exhaustive audit for statistically or substantively meaningful positive findings previously passed over. Every table was pulled live and every local data file read: bench_labels 124 rows, bench_outcomes 54, bench_records 5, interaction_events 342, study_runs 62, pilot_progress 27, armb_progress 18, guide_downloads 0, plus four local CSV/TSV files. All computation stdlib only, Fisher's exact two-sided from the hypergeometric distribution, Wilson score intervals on every proportion.

  **F1. PER-CONDITION DISCRIMINATION, ALL FIVE CONDITIONS, RECONFIRMED AND NOW WITH INTERVALS.** bench_labels.conditions is a five-key object on 108 of 124 labels. Every condition separates a Ready read from a not-Ready read at 14 of 14 against a much lower base rate, and every one clears significance by a wide margin. accountability_support 14/14 (100%, 95% CI 78.5 to 100.0) against 10/94 (10.6%), p = 1.4e-11. reasoning_traceability against 24/94 (25.5%), p = 6.92e-08. temporal_reconstructability against 23/94 (24.5%), p = 4.37e-08. cold_reviewer_clarity against 31/94 (33.0%), p = 1.19e-06. basis_identification against 36/94 (38.3%), p = 6.71e-06. **THE ORDERING IS ITSELF A FINDING AND HAS NEVER BEEN REPORTED**: accountability_support is the most discriminating condition by three orders of magnitude and basis_identification the least, which means the five conditions are not interchangeable and a shortened instrument would not be equivalent to the full one.

  **F2. STRUCTURED AGAINST UNSTRUCTURED REVIEW, RECOMPUTED AND THE EFFECT IS SMALLER THAN PREVIOUSLY RECORDED.** bench_labels.mode splits 108 structured against 16 unstructured on the same records with disjoint rater sets. Structured flags 94 of 108 = 87.0% (95% CI 79.4 to 92.1) from 21 raters; unstructured flags 8 of 16 = 50.0% (95% CI 28.0 to 72.0) from 3 raters. Fisher two-sided p = 0.00148, ratio 1.7x. **THE EARLIER TRACKER ENTRY RECORDED 69.4% AGAINST 6.2% AND 11.1x AT p = 1.57e-06. THAT IS NOT WHAT THE CURRENT DATA GIVES.** The earlier figure was computed on a Gap-only numerator; this one counts every flagged read, which is the comparison the claim actually needs. The corrected effect is real, significant and much smaller. It stays out of every peer-reviewed manuscript for the same reason as before: mode was not randomly assigned and the unstructured arm is 3 raters and 16 labels.

  **F3. THE ADVERSE-OUTCOME ASSOCIATION IS DOMAIN-SPECIFIC, AND THE FOIL SIDE IS NULL ON THIS CODING TOO.** Employment, n=22: flagged 7/9 adverse = 77.8% (95% CI 45.3 to 93.7) against Ready 2/13 = 15.4%, Fisher p = 0.00732, odds ratio 19.25. Public records, n=32: flagged 10/14 = 71.4% (95% CI 45.4 to 88.3) against Ready 10/18 = 55.6%, p = 0.4709, odds ratio 2.00. **This is a third independent coding of the FOIL corpus returning null, after the appellate-outcome specification check at p = 1.000.** It is not a discovery of a hidden positive; it is confirmation that the boundary condition reported in Section 5.6 is real rather than an artefact of one coding choice. Reported because a sweep that only records the findings that help is not a sweep.

  **F4. CROSS-VENDOR AGREEMENT IS NOT UNIFORM ACROSS RECORDS, AND THAT IS A STRONGER CLAIM THAN THE MEAN.** Latest full 15-record run, 2026-08-10: 8 of 15 records reach 100% three-vendor agreement, 6 sit at 67%, 1 at 50%. Mean 83.3%, median 100.0%. The disagreement is carried by a minority of records rather than spread evenly, which means the headline percentage understates how reproducible the instrument is on the records where it is reproducible at all. **A CORRECTION MADE DURING THE SWEEP**: the first pass took the most recent run, which held only 3 records and was a partial nightly. Using it would have reported a 3-record corpus as though it were 15. Restricted to full-corpus runs.

  **F5. RECORD-LEVEL STABILITY ACROSS CONSECUTIVE NIGHTLY RUNS, ENTIRELY NEW.** Six of fifteen records, acc_mod, ai_weak, comp_weak, foil_weak, hr_weak2 and weak, reached 100% three-vendor agreement in **every one of the last ten full runs**. Zero records disagreed in every run. Stability across independent runs on independent vendors is a different and more durable reproducibility claim than a single nightly mean, and no script in the repository had ever computed it.

  **F6. DOWNLOAD DEMAND IS INTERNATIONAL WHILE GATE TRAFFIC IS NOT.** 159 downloads across 13 countries: US 98, Germany 22, Poland 11, Singapore 8, China 5, France 4, Canada 3, Netherlands 2, and five more. Gate views are 93 of 95 United States. **The ungated assets reach an audience the gated funnel never touches.** By asset: Standard 51, Rapid Review Card 29, Employment guide 24, International 21, Fair Housing 19. That the International edition outdraws Fair Housing, on a site whose traffic is overwhelmingly US, is a demand signal for the international market that no campaign has been run at.

  **F7. TRAINING RETENTION IS FLAT, NOT DECAYING.** Module completions by index: 5, 5, 4, 4, 3, 4. Module 1 to Module 6 retention is 4 of 5 = 80%. The usual shape for a free self-paced course with no reminders loses most people after module 1. n=5 is far below any robust threshold and the figure is recorded with that stated rather than presented as a rate.

  **WHAT WAS SEARCHED AND FOUND EMPTY**: guide_downloads holds 0 rows, so all download evidence comes from interaction_events; reviewer-eval holds 0 rows because the instrument shipped yesterday; bench_records holds 5 rows and carries no outcome field to analyse. No further unanalysed structure remains in any table.

  **DISPOSITION.** F1 belongs in the detection paper and partly already sits in Section 5.4; the condition ordering is new and should be added. F5 belongs in the reproducibility section. F6 belongs in the acquisition prospectus, not a manuscript. F2 stays out of every manuscript and its earlier overstated figure is corrected here. F3 negative stays in the FOIL paper's limitations. F7 is an anecdote at n=5 and goes nowhere until the denominator grows.
- 2026-08-10 (question answered against code: does a survey-only completion collect what an IP sale needs): Owner asked whether the reviewer programme collects the data needed for the IP sale if a person only completes the record-check survey. Four paths traced through the deployed endpoints rather than described from the summary document. **PATH A, REVIEWER EVALUATION WITH THE CERTIFICATE BOX LEFT UNTICKED: HALF. IT COLLECTS MARKET EVIDENCE AND NO TRANSFERABLE CONTACT.** The whole identity block in api/reviewer-eval.js sits inside `if (wantsCert)`, so an untickd submission writes ONE row, the anonymous answers, and no pilot_contacts row at all. No name, no email, and critically no consent_transfer, which is the field that makes a contact record part of the asset rather than a list that dies with the seller. This is by design and the design is correct: joining the two would destroy the honesty of question 2. But it means the untickd path yields a market-description data point and zero pipeline. **PATH B, EVALUATION WITH THE CERTIFICATE TICKED: BOTH.** Row 1 anonymous answers; row 2 pilot_contacts source=reviewer-cert carrying name, email, printed title and the completion code, with consent_transfer true. That is the only path in the reviewer programme that produces a transferable contact. **PATH C, THE FULL RECORD CHECK ON org-pilot: THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE IN THE PROGRAMME AND IT HAS NEVER BEEN RUN.** It writes a pilot_contacts row carrying identity from the access gate plus records_run, the routing distribution and per-condition pass/needs-attention/fail counts, with consent_contact, consent_transfer and consent_named_org. It stores no record text. That row is field-usage evidence, an actual organization running the standard on its own documents, which is the one thing a buyer prices above everything else. orgpilot-stats returns 0 sessions and 0 records, so this path has produced nothing to date. **PATH D, THE ZERO-FIELD INSTANT TEST ADDED 2026-08-08: DELIBERATELY COLLECTS NOTHING.** It calls /api/review only, does not touch /api/org-pilot, does not increment the session counter and writes no row anywhere. That was the correct trade when it was built, to prove the instrument before asking for an identity, but it means the highest-traffic entry point on the pilot page is invisible in every diligence figure. **THE GAP, STATED PLAINLY**: the two paths a casual visitor is most likely to take, an untickd evaluation and the zero-field test, both produce either no contact or no row at all. The certificate tick is the only bridge from anonymous participation to a transferable record, and it is optional and off by default.
- 2026-08-10 (Option B implemented: optional peer-recommendation incentive on the reviewer evaluation, with a third fully isolated row): Owner directed capture of transferable contacts from evaluation-only completions. **THE PROBLEM IT SOLVES, RESTATED FROM THE CODE**: an evaluation submitted with the certificate box unticked wrote one row, the anonymous answers, and no pilot_contacts row at all, so it produced a market data point and no consent_transfer and therefore nothing that travels with the assets. **FRONTEND**: an incentive block sits above the submit button offering a LinkedIn Peer Reviewer Recommendation for contributing to the research baseline. Ticking it reveals full name, work email and LinkedIn profile URL through a max-height and opacity transition rather than a display toggle, so the block does not jump the page under a thumb on a phone, and it scrolls itself into view after the transition. The required consent micro-copy sits beneath the fields verbatim as specified. All three fields persist to localStorage alongside the existing certificate fields and restore on return. **BACKEND, THREE ROWS, AND THE ISOLATION IS ENFORCED BY OMISSION RATHER THAN BY A FILTER**: row 1 the anonymous answers to interaction_events; row 2 the certificate identity to pilot_contacts as reviewer-cert; row 3 the incentive contact to pilot_contacts as reviewer-eval-incentive carrying name, work email, LinkedIn URL and three consent flags. The incentive payload contains no completion code, no evaluation id, no answer, no answered count, no sector, no org size, no role and no modules-completed value. Nothing written on it appears on the evaluation row, so there is no foreign key, no join key and no shared identifier of any kind; the only thing in common is a coarse timestamp, and a timestamp shared by every submission in the same minute is not an identifier. Verified field by field against the deployed source. **THE LINKEDIN FIELD IS SANITISED TO A HOST ALLOWLIST RATHER THAN STORED AS TYPED**, because a URL field that later renders in any dashboard is an injection surface. A bare handle normalises to linkedin.com/in/, a bare domain gains https and www, a regional subdomain keeps its own host, and anything that is not a LinkedIn host or that contains a quote, angle bracket or backslash is dropped rather than stored. Seven inputs tested live: four accepted and normalised correctly, three dropped including evil.com and a javascript: payload. **TWO DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED DURING THE TEST.** First, a regional subdomain was rewritten to www.uk.linkedin.com, which is not a host that resolves; www is now prepended only where there is no subdomain. Second, and more consequential, contact validation ran AFTER the test-mode early return, so a malformed name or email came back 200 whenever a test tag was present. The validation the audit exists to verify was unreachable by the audit. Validation now runs before the branch and all four malformed cases return 400 under a test tag. **END TO END ON PRODUCTION, ALL GUARDED**: unticked submission answers 9 of 9 with certificate false and incentive false; ticked submission returns incentive true with a normalised LinkedIn URL; four validation cases return 400; research baseline holds 0 reviewer-eval rows; nine routes including /train, /training, /pilot and /guide all return 200 with no breaking change. **ONE WORDING DECISION RECORDED**: the recommendation is offered for the contribution to the research baseline and the page states that it does not assert anything about professional performance, which has not been observed. A recommendation that claimed otherwise would be an endorsement written about work nobody saw.
- 2026-08-10 (evaluation funnel now measured end to end; programme summary revised): Owner asked to see the incentive block and to make certain three things are measured: how many click the evaluation, how many provide the needed information, and how many complete the survey. **THE FIRST OF THOSE THREE WAS NOT BEING RECORDED AT ALL.** api/reviewer-eval served the question set on a GET and logged nothing, so the earliest event the system could see was a completed submission. A page nobody finished and a page nobody opened were indistinguishable, which means the click-through number the owner asked for did not exist. The GET now writes an eval-view row carrying the source tag, country, user agent and computed is_mobile, guarded against deploy checks and owner previews, verified live: a verify-tagged open writes zero rows. **THE PAGE NOW PASSES ITS SOURCE TAG THROUGH TO THE OPEN**, so ?src=linkedin and ?src=email separate in the funnel instead of every open arriving untagged. The submit handler was refactored to reuse the same parsed value rather than parsing the query string twice. **asset-stats GAINED reviewer_evaluation_funnel WITH TEN FIELDS**: opened, submitted, completed_all_questions, mean_questions_answered, contacts_captured, contacts_via_recommendation, contacts_via_certificate, and the three conversion rates open_to_submit, submit_to_contact and open_to_contact. contacts_captured is the figure that matters for a sale, and it is split by which route produced it so the incentive block can be judged against the certificate tick on its own numbers. **ONE LIMITATION STATED INSIDE THE PAYLOAD RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE ASSUMED**: opened counts page opens and not distinct people, because the evaluation page carries no per-person key and inventing one would mean fingerprinting the reader, which the rest of this system deliberately does not do. The payload also states that the answer rows and the contact rows sit in different tables with no shared identifier, so the two counts cannot be joined to say which respondent gave which answers. **SCREENSHOTS RENDERED** at 430px mobile viewport, deviceScaleFactor 2, with the API stubbed to the real question set so the page renders exactly as production does: research/shot_incentive_closed.png and research/shot_incentive_open.png. **PROGRAMME SUMMARY REVISED** at research/Reviewer_Program_Summary_2026-08-09.md, now 2,139 words: a new section on the optional exchange covering why it exists, what the recommendation does and does not assert, and the LinkedIn host allowlist; a new measurement section with the ten-field table and the statement that the open count did not exist before 2026-08-10; the two-unlinked-rows table extended to three rows with the isolation-by-omission explanation; the documentation table extended with the incentive row; and the open item rewritten so a zero on every funnel metric reads as the expected state for an instrument that shipped yesterday rather than as a measured result. **LIVE READING**: every funnel metric reads 0.
- 2026-08-10 (evaluation funnel wired into pilot-status.html; contact routes on the evaluation clarified): Owner asked whether the evaluation carries a registration link or whether a respondent must tick the LinkedIn recommendation, and asked for the funnel stats surfaced on the public status page. **ANSWER ON THE CONTACT ROUTES: THERE ARE TWO, BOTH OPTIONAL, AND NEITHER IS A REGISTRATION LINK.** The evaluation page carries no registration link and never sends a respondent to the gate. Contact details can enter through the certificate tick, want-cert, which asks name, printed title, work email and organization; or through the recommendation tick, want-rec, which asks name, work email and LinkedIn URL. Both are unticked by default and neither is required to submit. The only required tick on the page is consent_research, which governs anonymous use of the answers and captures no identity. A respondent can therefore complete all nine questions and leave nothing contactable, which is exactly the gap the recommendation block was added to close and is why the two routes are counted separately in the funnel. **PANEL ADDED TO pilot-status.html**, placed above Contributor Confirmations and wired into the existing refresh cycle. Four stage cards: opened, submitted, answered all nine, contacts captured. A scaled bar chart headed "Where the funnel loses people" so the drop between stages is what the eye reads rather than the absolute counts. A note line carrying all three conversion rates and the split between the recommendation route and the certificate route, so the incentive block can be judged on its own numbers rather than as a blended figure. **THE EMPTY STATE SAYS WHAT A ZERO MEANS**: "No evaluation activity recorded yet. The instrument opened on 9 August 2026 and the open counter on 10 August, so a zero here is the expected state rather than a measured result." Without that line a visitor reads four zeros as measured failure. **THE LIMITATION IS ON THE PAGE, NOT ONLY IN THE PAYLOAD**: the panel's own subtitle states that answers and contact details sit in different tables with no shared identifier and cannot be joined, and the note states that opens count page opens rather than distinct people because the page carries no per-person key. **VALIDATION**: inline scripts pass node --check, no dangling element ids, zero em-dashes, panel confirmed live on production, and a rendered screenshot at 900px is filed at research/shot_status_funnel.png.
- 2026-08-10 (programme summary restructured: the contact-collection answer moved to the front): Owner said the document left it unclear when contacts get collected. The answer was in the document but scattered across three sections that a reader had to assemble: the pathway table, the optional-exchange section and the row-isolation table. **A NEW SECTION NOW OPENS THE DOCUMENT, BEFORE "WHAT IT IS"**, headed "When contacts get collected", stating four moments and only four in one table: registration to unlock modules 2 to 6, which is the only compulsory one; the certificate tick; the recommendation tick; and the full record check on /pilot. **THE LINE THAT RESOLVES THE CONFUSION IS STATED ON ITS OWN**: answering the nine questions collects nothing about the respondent. A person can open the evaluation, answer all nine, submit, and leave no name, no email and no contact record. That is the design and it is the reason the answers are worth having, because question 2 asks whether their own employer has a formal second reader and a name attached to that answer buys a careful answer rather than a true one. **A FOUR-CASE OUTCOME LIST** was added so the tick combinations are not left to inference: neither ticked gives one anonymous row; certificate gives an answer row plus a contact row with a completion code; recommendation gives an answer row plus a contact row with a LinkedIn URL; both give an answer row plus TWO contact rows, which is why the funnel counts the routes separately rather than as one blended figure. **THE REGISTRATION QUESTION IS ANSWERED EXPLICITLY** rather than left as an absence: the evaluation page carries no registration link, never sends anyone to a gate, and its only required tick is consent to anonymous research use, which captures no identity. **TWO STRUCTURAL CORRECTIONS IN THE SAME PASS**: the pathway table's trailing note now states that steps 4 and 5 can be taken without steps 1 to 3, since the evaluation is a standalone link; and the row-isolation heading read "Two unlinked rows" while the table beneath it listed three, so it now reads "Three unlinked rows". A stray double horizontal rule left by an earlier edit was removed. Document now 2,534 words, zero em-dashes, zero banned vocabulary.
- 2026-08-10 (country of reviewer now tracked and surfaced at every funnel stage): Owner asked to keep track of the reviewer's country. **THE DATA WAS ALREADY THERE AND HAD NEVER BEEN SHOWN.** The two-letter edge code has been written on all four evaluation row types since they were built, verified field by field: eval-view for the open, reviewer-eval for the answers, reviewer-cert for the certificate contact, and reviewer-eval-incentive for the recommendation contact. Nothing needed collecting; it needed surfacing. **asset-stats GAINED FOUR FIELDS**: countries_opened, countries_submitted, countries_contacts and distinct_countries_opened, each a per-country count sorted by size. Reported per stage rather than as one total, because a country that opens and never submits is a different signal from one that converts and a single figure hides both. **pilot-status GAINED A COUNTRY OF REVIEWER TABLE** with a Countries Reached card, one row per country carrying a scaled bar for opens plus submitted and contact columns, so the drop-off by geography is readable at a glance. Contacts render in the ready colour when non-zero and muted when zero, so a country that produces contacts is visually distinct from one that only browses. **ONE THING DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT, AND THE REASON IS ON THE PAGE**: answers are not cross-tabulated by country. With a handful of responses, "the one respondent from Iceland says their employer has no second reader" is a re-identification, and the entire instrument depends on that being impossible. The endpoint returns counts per country and never answers per country, and the country_note field states this so a future reader does not add it as an obvious improvement. **A SECOND CAVEAT STATED RATHER THAN LEFT TO ASSUMPTION**: the code comes from the network edge at the moment of the request, so it reports where the reader is sitting and not where their employer is. A compliance officer answering from an airport is counted at the airport. **PROGRAMME SUMMARY UPDATED** with the four new metrics and all three caveats. **VERIFIED LIVE**: the four fields return on production, currently empty arrays with zero distinct countries, which is correct for an instrument with no traffic yet. A populated render is filed at research/shot_status_country.png so the panel can be judged on how it reads with data rather than only on its empty state.

- 2026-08-11 (Alexandria Davis recommendation rewritten into the house format): Owner said the recommendation was a different format from the one every other reviewer has received. Correct. **THE HOUSE FORMAT WAS ALREADY ESTABLISHED ACROSS 21 FILES** named LinkedIn_Recommendation_<Name>.md, and the draft ignored it. The established shape is a guardrail header in italics, then three body paragraphs and a bare signature: paragraph one opens "I had the pleasure of working with <Name> as an independent reviewer on the Records Review Study, an international panel of professionals evaluating administrative and workplace records" and states that they "completed the full 24-record review with rigor, care, and genuinely independent judgment"; paragraph two gives their specific background and why that background matters for this particular work, closing on how it showed in their reviews; paragraph three says they "would be an asset to any organization working on" their fields and "I recommend her without reservation"; then "Phillip Wikes" alone. **REWRITTEN TO MATCH AND FILED WHERE THE OTHERS LIVE** at research/LinkedIn_Recommendation_Alexandria_Davis.md. Verified against the pattern on nine structural checks, all passing, including the three-paragraph count and the bare signature. **THE BARE SIGNATURE IS A BLIND PROTECTION, NOT A STYLE CHOICE**, and the earlier draft would have got this wrong by omission: every Arm B guardrail header carries the instruction never to sign "Creator, JRS" on a public recommendation, because RR-108 has not finished and other blind recruits and their networks can read it. The posted text carries none of JRS, Justification, reconstruct, five condition, five review, verified key, comparison or Creator. **HER PARAGRAPH TWO IS BUILT ON WHAT THE RECORD ACTUALLY HOLDS**: responsible AI and fairness in financial systems, where the question is rarely whether a decision was made and almost always whether the file behind it holds up when someone looks, which is the discipline the study asks for. It also records that she asked whether participation would be represented as endorsement before agreeing to take part, because that is a fact about her judgment and it is the reason her guardrail header carries a second warning that her permission must be explicit before posting. **THE TWO-VERSION DRAFT IS GONE.** One format, one file, matching the other twenty one. The completion message now carries the standard text inline for her to approve or change, and its file table points at the standard file.
- 2026-08-11 (conversion diagnostic rerun on live data; the answer to two of four questions is that every measured visitor was a crawler): Owner asked for CONVERSION_DIAGNOSTIC_REPORT.md rebuilt from production with field abandonment, device split, link opens and the overall funnel. Pulled all 344 interaction_events rows through the anon view plus the eight live stats endpoints. **THE CENTRAL FINDING IS THAT SINCE FIELD-LEVEL AND DEVICE TELEMETRY SHIPPED ON 2026-08-09 15:45 UTC, ALL SIX GATE VIEWS HAVE BEEN SEARCH ENGINE CRAWLERS: three Googlebot, two GoogleOther, one Baiduspider. Zero humans.** The instruments fired correctly on every request they saw; there were no people to see. **THE SINGLE field_touched EVENT EVER RECORDED IS BAIDUSPIDER-RENDER**, which executes page JS, focuses f-name during layout and fires the ping one second after its own page view. Human field-abandonment observations: zero, so no share column was computed and none was estimated. **A LIMITATION WAS STATED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER**: field_touched fires once per page session on FIRST focus and then sets a sessionStorage flag, so it records an entry point, not a depth, and cannot answer "which field did they reach before dropping off" even when traffic returns. The fix is a last-field-touched ping or a sendBeacon on visibilitychange, roughly fifteen lines, and the recommendation is explicitly NOT to build it yet: two instruments already sit live over a window with no human traffic. **THE 80 PERCENT MOBILE FIGURE PUBLISHED BY /api/asset-stats IS A CRAWLER ARTIFACT AND MUST NOT GO IN ANY DILIGENCE DOCUMENT.** Of ten device-flagged rows, seven are crawlers, and Googlebot's smartphone UA classifies as mobile, which is what put the mobile count at 8. Removing crawlers leaves three rows, all one person: iPhone iOS 18.7, LinkedIn in-app browser, US, honor code H-2026-01, three loads across four minutes on 2026-08-10. **CONVERSION BY DEVICE IS UNDEFINED, NOT ZERO**, and structurally so: the denominator has carried a device flag only since 2026-08-09 and the numerator is one registration dated 2026-08-04. Registration one is permanently unclassifiable; two onward will be attributable because the POST path now writes is_mobile onto the contact row. **LINK OPENS MUST BE REPORTED ON SENT LINKS, NOT ISSUED ONES**: honor is 1 of 1 sent opened and accepted, which is 100 percent and 100 percent, while asset-stats' 2.9 percent divides by 34 issued of which 33 have never been sent. Contributor 20 issued, 0 sent, 0 opened. Blind re-read 3 issued, 0 sent. Reviewer evaluation suite: every stage a true zero, built and never sent. **honor-cert AT 0 DOWNLOADS IS A CHANNEL MISMATCH, NOT A DROP-OFF**, because the certificate goes out as an email attachment; the actionable point is that an attachment produces no open signal at all and a link does, so certificates should be delivered as honor-cert links if the purpose is evidencing engagement for a sale. **TWO DEFECTS FOUND IN THE METRICS THEMSELVES AND FIXED BEFORE REPORTING OFF THEM.** api/gate-stats.js counted every gate-view row as a form view without checking type, so the field_touched row landed in the conversion denominator; that was one row today and would have compounded into a phantom abandonment for every reader who touched a field. Fixed with a type check, and touches now report separately as field_touches, field_touches_by_field and started_form_pct. access.html listed f-title in the field-touch FIELDS array, but it is a hidden input set in script and never surfaced, so it cannot receive focus and could never have fired; removed. **THE CORRECTED FUNNEL IS 101 FORM VIEWS AND 1 REGISTRATION, 0.99 PERCENT**, not the 102 the endpoint was reporting; support route 81 views 1 registration, guide route 20 views 0 registrations. Since the 2026-08-09 report: +6 views, +0 registrations, and the numerator has not moved in seven days. **THE 101 IS AN UPPER BOUND ON HUMAN VIEWS AND A LOOSE ONE**: the deploy-check guard did not ship until 2026-08-10 13:23 and UA capture until 2026-08-09 15:45, so 95 of the 101 cannot be separated from crawler or owner validation traffic, 32 of 101 arrived within 60 seconds of the previous view including one-second runs on 2026-08-08 while the gate was being redeployed, and the crawler share in the only observable window is 100 percent. Never publish it without the note. **THE MOST ACTIONABLE PATTERN IS THE CONTRAST WITH EVERY OTHER SURFACE**: 40 one-click endorsements, 160 artifact downloads, 7 of 7 training completions, 34 full-set research completers, against 1 gate registration. Every surface that asks for a click converts and every surface that asks for a form does not, so the gate's problem right now is traffic and not friction, and no form optimization addresses it. LinkedIn is 61.4 percent of gate views and the only human device observed came through the LinkedIn in-app browser on a phone, which is also the environment that previously broke the terms link, so that is where both the traffic push and the testing belong. Report kept OFF main deliberately: it carries internal conversion-failure and crawler analysis and the repo-root reports have never been deployed. Only the two code fixes went to production.
- 2026-08-11 (the gate-stats fix shipped and did nothing, caught by reading production rather than trusting the deploy): The type check added to keep field_touched pings out of form_views was logically correct and passed node --check, and changed nothing live. **THE EVENTS QUERY SELECTS source, payload, created_at ONLY, SO e.type WAS ALWAYS UNDEFINED AND THE GUARD NEVER FIRED.** Caught by hitting /api/gate-stats after the deploy and seeing form_views still 102 with the new field_touches reading 0, which is the only reason it was caught at all: a syntax check cannot see a missing column and there is no build step or test suite on this repo to catch it either. Added type to the select and redeployed. **VERIFIED LIVE AFTER THE SECOND DEPLOY: form_views 101, form_views_guide 20, abandoned 100, field_touches 1, field_touches_by_field [f-name 1], started_form_pct 1.** The lesson to keep: on a repo with no tests, a deploy is not evidence and the endpoint has to be read back before any number computed from it is reported.

No shared join key. Nothing in this change touched that file. **A DEFECT THE CHANGE ITSELF INTRODUCED WAS FIXED RATHER THAN LEFT**: the OpenGraph card and page title still promised a Free Record Defensibility Check, which the campaign route no longer offers, and access.html is the most-shared URL on the site with 62 of 101 views arriving from LinkedIn. og:title, og:description, twitter:title and twitter:description rewritten to the panel framing, and document.title set per mode so the campaign screen stops saying Register. **THE LINKEDIN CARD FOR ALREADY-POSTED CAMPAIGN LINKS WILL STILL SHOW THE OLD TEXT UNTIL RE-SCRAPED THROUGH POST INSPECTOR**, which is owner-side and now more urgent than it was. Also removed two orphans: the empty registry-live placeholder, which nothing writes to now that the live count moved under the buttons, and the unguarded document-level Enter handler, which would have run submit() against hidden empty inputs on the campaign route. **ONE CONSEQUENCE STATED PLAINLY FOR THE OWNER**: campaign traffic no longer produces a transferable contact at the gate. Declared crawlers are now excluded from the device split and from evaluation opens, with crawler_rows_excluded and opened_crawlers_excluded published rather than the exclusion being silent. **THE N>=30 DISCLOSURE FLOOR IS SET BEFORE THE FIRST RESPONSE ARRIVES, WHICH IS THE ONLY TIME IT CAN HONESTLY BE SET.** Below 30 submissions, breakdowns by country, sector, role and organization size return empty with breakdowns_released false and a note stating the threshold and the reason. Sector, role and org-size breakdowns were added at the same time, gated, so they exist and are simply withheld. Opens are deliberately NOT gated: a page open carries no answer and no identity and cannot re-identify a respondent, and the distinct-country scalars survive for the same reason. pilot-status.html was updated to say the columns are withheld rather than empty, because otherwise the dashboard reads zeros as a finding. **ONE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE OWNER: THE SITE NOW HAS NO CONTACT CAPTURE IN FRONT OF ANYTHING.** The campaign gate went on 2026-08-11 and the guide gate has now followed. Built api/panel-stats.js, an anon-key GET returning reviewers, completers, countries, continents plus the components. **ITS ARITHMETIC WAS PROVED AGAINST count_participants.py BEFORE SHIPPING, NOT AFTER**: 55 reviewers, 34 completers, 46 registered, from graded 35 + 4 Study-004 experts + 16 bench reviewers, matching the canonical script exactly. Only two constants remain and both are identity resolutions rather than statistics: E-09/E-12/E-13 are the same humans as Arm A completers and would otherwise double-count, and E-11 has one label and no identity so it cannot be resolved. **COUNTRIES AND CONTINENTS CANNOT BE COMPUTED AND ARE NOT PRETENDED TO BE.** No country is stored in any anon-readable table; pilot_progress and armb_progress carry a code and a read count, and the identities carrying a country live in RLS-locked bench_experts. The reviewer countries exist only in the private transcribed roster. So they stay maintained constants and the endpoint publishes geo_source "transcribed", geo_as_of and a note saying how to rederive, because a figure that cannot refresh itself should say so rather than sit beside three that can and borrow their credibility. **SIX PAGES WIRED VIA data-panel ATTRIBUTES WITH THE SHIPPED NUMBER LEFT IN THE MARKUP AS THE FALLBACK**: access.html, reviewer/index.html (prose and the stat tile), org-pilot.html, investigator-guides.html, research.html and the acquisition page. **THREE BEHAVIOURS WERE TESTED, NOT ASSUMED**, using values deliberately unlike anything hardcoded: with the endpoint returning 99/88/77/6/66 every one of the six pages rendered those numbers, so population is real rather than coincidental; with the endpoint returning 500 every page held its shipped values; and with a junk payload of 0, null and a string, the typeof-number-and-positive guard rejected all three while still applying the one valid field. **A TESTING MISTAKE WORTH RECORDING**: the first run appeared to show population failing, and the cause was my own harness, since Playwright matches the last-registered route first and a catch-all registered after the specific stub was shadowing it. Registry buttons: "Join the Registry" became "Pressure-test the standard (4-min evaluation)" on all six cards across investigator-guides.html, index.html and decision-reconstruction-risk.html, because the same card component on three pages must not drift apart. The guides page took the specified src=field_guides; index and DRR keep src=home and src=drr so attribution stays meaningful rather than being flattened. Endpoints unchanged as instructed, so the endorsement is still recorded on the way through. Micro-copy: the checkmark was restored to the trust line, since this prompt specifies it and supersedes the version issued earlier today without it. **CRAWLER FILTER WIDENED AND ONE GAP CLOSED HONESTLY**: googlebot, bingbot, baiduspider, yandexbot, duckduckbot and applebot are now named explicitly, and the filter was extended to the public artifact download count. That exposed a real limitation, which is that api/dl.js never captured a user agent, so download rows could not be tested at all. Added capture to all three download row shapes, and asset-stats now publishes downloads_crawlers_excluded alongside downloads_predating_user_agent_capture so nobody reads the filter as total when older rows cannot be tested. **PRE-EXISTING PROSE DRIFT NOTED AND NOT SILENTLY FIXED**: index.html contains banned vocabulary from before today ("in summary", "leverage", "frequently", "Designed for") which is outside this change and is flagged for a deliberate pass rather than folded into a telemetry commit.
- 2026-08-11 (REGRESSION SHIPPED AND FIXED: access.html lost its entire head; index prose fingerprints cleared): Owner sent a phone screenshot of a tiny narrow card floating in dead space and asked why. **THE PAGE HAD NO <head> AT ALL. I DESTROYED IT AND DEPLOYED IT.** The CSS cleanup earlier today ran css = s[s.index('<style>'):s.index('</style>')] and then wrote newcss + body, which silently discarded everything before <style>: the DOCTYPE, <html>, <head>, charset, title, robots noindex, description, favicon, canonical, all OpenGraph and Twitter tags, the font preconnects and the Google Fonts stylesheet. **THE VISIBLE SYMPTOM WAS CAUSED BY THE MISSING VIEWPORT META**: with no width=device-width, mobile Safari lays out at its 980px default and scales the result down, which is exactly the shrunken 48-percent-width card in the screenshot. The OpenGraph loss was the more expensive half and was invisible: access.html is the most-shared URL on the site and every campaign share card would have rendered as a bare grey row. **HOW IT GOT THROUGH: EVERY CHECK I RAN WAS A CONTENT CHECK.** node --check passed because the inline script was intact, the headless render passed because Chromium tolerates a headless document and applies no mobile viewport scaling at a fixed 390px viewport, and the curl greps checked for strings inside the body. Nothing asserted that the document still had a head. Restored the head from 949bbde with the title and description updated to the page's actual purpose, then added a structural assertion over all 66 HTML files checking doctype, viewport, head, body and html close: all 66 now pass. Re-verified on a real mobile emulation profile (isMobile, hasTouch, dpr 3, 390x844): layout viewport 390, card 346px at 89 percent of screen, Bodoni Moda resolving, no horizontal scroll. **INDEX PROSE FIXED**: four "Designed For" section labels became "Who uses it", five "designed for" prose openers rewritten, and "highest-leverage" became "strongest", plus the same leverage phrasing in jrsstandard.html and reference/ai-verification-controls/index.html. **FOUR MATCHES WERE LEFT IN PLACE DELIBERATELY AND THE REASON MATTERS MORE THAN THE RULE**: "frequently unprofessional" in index.html and jrsstandard.html sits inside the sample deficient record the whole method exists to critique, "frequently made disparaging remarks" is inside a quoted witness statement, and the two "Frequently:" strings in training.html are Likert scale labels that the localStorage restore matches on with b.textContent.trim() === val, so editing them would silently break saved survey progress for everyone who has already answered. A style rule does not outrank a teaching example or a persistence key.

All 66 HTML files pass the new structural assertion. **STATE READ FROM THE LIVE ENDPOINTS AT 2026-08-11, SO THESE ARE MEASUREMENTS AND NOT RECOLLECTIONS.** Panel: 55 reviewers, 34 completers, 16 countries, 5 continents, 46 registered, now served by /api/panel-stats and rendered on six pages with the shipped values as fallback. Gate: 124 form views against 1 registration and 1 field touch, and the form itself no longer exists on either route, so that denominator is now historical rather than live. Evaluation: 0 opened, 0 submitted, 0 contacts, disclosure floor set at 30 and not released. Downloads: 167 public artifacts of which 70 are field guides across 7 countries, 0 crawler rows excluded so far because user-agent capture only began today and all 167 predate it. Device: 26 mobile, 0 desktop, 7 crawler rows excluded. Named engagement: honor 34 issued with 1 sent, opened and accepted; contributor 20 issued and 0 sent. **THE SHAPE OF THE DAY, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE ARGUMENT A BUYER WILL BE MAKING**: every gate on the site was removed today, the campaign gate first and the guide gate second, on evidence that the two of them returned 1 registration against roughly 120 opens and 0 against 20. Contact capture now happens in exactly one place, the evaluation's optional incentive block, and it is paid for with a recommendation or a certificate rather than demanded before entry. That surface has never been sent to anyone and reads 0 at every stage. **THE SINGLE HIGHEST-VALUE OWNER ACTION IS THEREFORE TO SEND THE EVALUATION**, because every measurement system built today is now instrumented, filtered, thresholded and empty. **OWNER-SIDE OPEN ITEMS CARRIED FORWARD**: re-scrape both campaign links through LinkedIn Post Inspector, since posted cards still show the pre-2026-08-11 OpenGraph; add Alexandria Davis to the honor roster if she is to receive one, which needs a citation and a link and is a recognition decision rather than a count correction; get her permission before posting her recommendation and ask separately about V-AI-31 at 0 reads; send Stacyann Young's honor materials and certificate and the R1 blind-recheck link once she names her attorney reader; 33 honor links and 20 contributor links still held pending Arm B close, and RR-108 has still not finished; file the JRS and DRR trademarks; set ANON_CODES for RR-130 and RR-132. **PROCESS CHANGE ADOPTED TODAY AND WORTH KEEPING**: two defects shipped and were caught only by reading production afterwards, the gate-stats guard that never fired because its column was not selected and the access.html head that was deleted by a slicing script. Both passed syntax checks and content greps. The rule that follows is that a deploy is not evidence, and any change to a file's structure needs a structural assertion rather than a check that the words are still there.
- 2026-08-11 (endorsements-per-day chart added to pilot-status.html): Owner asked for a visual count of endorsements per day on the public status dashboard. **THE SERIES DID NOT EXIST AND HAD TO BE BUILT FIRST**: api/support-stats.js selected only the payload column from the source=eq.support rows, so no timestamp ever reached the client and no daily figure could be drawn. Added created_at to the select with an ascending order, and the endpoint now returns by_day plus days_span, days_with_activity, peak_day_endorsements, mean_per_active_day and a note. **THE SERIES IS DENSE, NOT SPARSE, AND THAT IS THE POINT**: every date between the first and last endorsement is emitted with a zero where nothing happened, because a sparse series drawn as a bar chart silently closes the gaps and makes a quiet stretch look busy. 2026-08-03 has zero endorsements and now renders as an empty column instead of vanishing. No deduplication is applied and none is needed: the endorsement is a bare GET with no form behind it, so one row is one click. The existing internal-smoke-test source filter still applies, so verify, test, selftest and deploytest clicks stay out. **CHART BUILT IN THE HOUSE PATTERN, NO LIBRARY, INLINE STYLES, DESIGN TOKENS ONLY**, matching the existing .chart and .cards blocks: four stat tiles (endorsements, best single day, days with activity, mean per active day) above a column chart in var(--ready-text) with var(--rule) for zero days, a thinned date axis that shows at most twelve labels so they cannot collide on a phone, a per-column title attribute carrying the exact date and count, and a sentence underneath stating span, active days, peak and the first and last dates. **LIVE VALUES AT BUILD TIME: 40 endorsements over 5 days, 4 of them active, peak 28 on 2026-07-31, mean 10.0 per active day, latest 2026-08-04.** Two defects were caught in review before deploy and fixed: the bar cells used flex 1 0 14px with no maximum, so a five-day series stretched each column to roughly 250px, capped at 46px with the identical cap applied to the axis cells so labels stay under their columns; and the loadSupport catch branch reset the other two charts but not this one, leaving it on Loading forever if the endpoint failed, so a failure state was wired. Verified at 900px and 390px: five columns rendered, titles correct, no horizontal scroll at either width. **THE NUMBERS ALSO ANSWER A QUESTION NOT ASKED: THE ENDORSEMENT CURVE IS NOT LIVE TRAFFIC.** The latest endorsement is 2026-08-04, a week old, with 28 of the 40 on a single day. The one-click endorsement pathway is not currently receiving clicks, which is the same finding as the empty evaluation funnel and has the same cause.
- 2026-08-11 (the one-click endorsement was recording NOTHING and is now fixed and verified end to end): Owner asked to confirm the one-click endorsement is active. **IT WAS NOT. NOTHING HAD RECORDED AN ENDORSEMENT SINCE 03:45 TODAY, AND I BROKE IT.** api/support.js stopped writing on 2026-08-02 and handed the write to the registration form, which api/access performed on submit. Replacing the campaign gate with a single evaluation CTA earlier today removed that form, and with it the only thing writing the row. Between those two changes every click 302'd to a screen reading "Your support is recorded" and recorded nothing. The last genuine endorsement is dated 2026-08-04T09:03Z. **THE PRECEDING TURN'S CHART MADE THIS VISIBLE AND I DID NOT READ IT AS A DEFECT**: the daily series showed the latest endorsement a week old with a flat tail, which was reported as "the pathway is not currently receiving clicks" when the correct reading was that the pathway was not currently recording them. The data was on screen and the wrong conclusion was drawn from it. **FIX**: the write is back on the bare GET in api/support.js, with the same payload shape as the pre-2026-08-02 rows so the daily series and country breakdown stay one continuous history rather than two incompatible halves. Campaign, referral tag and the two-letter edge country only. No name, no email, no IP, no user agent, no identifier of any kind. The redirect is issued whatever the database does, so a write failure never costs the reader their click. No registered flag is set, because nobody registers here and gate-stats uses that flag to tell a registration from a click. **VERIFIED IN FOUR STAGES AGAINST PRODUCTION**: a suppressed tag wrote nothing and the total held at 40; an unsuppressed e2echeck tag wrote a real row and the total moved to 41, proving the write path rather than assuming it; the purge helper removed that row and the total returned to 40; and the full redirect chain was walked for both campaigns across six referral tags, all twelve resolving correctly with campaign and src preserved into access.html. **THOSE TWELVE CHAIN CHECKS WROTE TWELVE REAL ROWS AND POLLUTED THE COUNT TO 52, WHICH IS THE EXACT ERROR I HAVE BEEN GUARDING AGAINST ALL SESSION.** They carried genuine src tags so no tag filter could reach them. Purged by an exact timestamp bracket instead, which is safe by construction here: the previous real endorsement is 2026-08-04T09:03Z, nothing was recorded in the interval because the write was broken, and all twelve landed inside a six second window. Verified back to 40 with the daily series restored to 28/2/9/0/1 and by_source back to its genuine distribution. **STANDING ADDITION TO THE PURGE HELPER**: source=support rows tagged e2echeck are now removed automatically, so the endorsement counter can be verified in future without permanently inflating it, which was the only reason the twelve real clicks were fired in the first place. **THE LESSON THAT GENERALISES: REMOVING A FORM REMOVES EVERYTHING THAT FORM WAS DOING.** The gate was removed as a conversion obstacle and it was also the write path for a separate counter on a separate page. Nothing in the change touched api/support.js, so nothing in the review of that change looked at it.
- 2026-08-11 (who was actually lost in the endorsement outage, and what was downloaded today): Owner asked whether this morning's clickers were captured and whether today's guides were downloaded. **NOBODY WAS CAPTURED, BUT THE LOSS IS ONE PERSON, NOT EIGHTEEN.** The endorsement write was broken, so zero endorsement rows exist for today. The clicks are recoverable from the gate-view rows access.html writes on load, which is a reliable 1:1 proxy because one /api/support click produces one page load and one row. Today shows 18 campaign gate views between 03:47 and 04:45, and **ALL 18 CARRY A SINGLE IDENTICAL USER AGENT**: iPhone, iOS 26.5.2, Chrome for iOS 151, US, mobile. One device, 18 loads across 58 minutes, alternating between rtkw and defend, with a load at 04:29:26 matching the timestamp on the owner's own screenshot. That is a person testing their own links, not eighteen visitors, and reporting it as eighteen lost endorsements would have overstated the loss by a factor of eighteen. **THE OUTAGE IS ALSO MUCH OLDER THAN THIS MORNING AND THAT MATTERS MORE.** api/support stopped writing directly on 2026-08-02 and handed the write to the registration form. Since that date there have been 99 campaign gate views, meaning 99 clicks that landed on the screen, against exactly 1 endorsement row written, the registered row dated 2026-08-04. So the count has been effectively frozen for nine days, not five hours, and the 40 total is a pre-2026-08-02 figure carried forward. Removing the form this morning did not start the problem, it removed the last surviving path through it. **NO BACKFILL WAS PERFORMED AND NONE SHOULD BE WITHOUT AN EXPLICIT DECISION**: writing 98 synthetic endorsement rows from gate-view proxies would put fabricated records into the dataset a buyer will audit, and the proxy is sound for counting but is not the same event. The gate-view rows remain available if the owner wants the reconstruction done deliberately and labelled as such. **TODAY'S GUIDE DOWNLOADS: 7 ROWS, OF WHICH 5 WERE MINE.** The block at 04:16:08 to 04:16:12 is exactly the five link shapes from my own gate-removal verification, one second apart, in the order the check ran, carrying international/email, employment/site, fairhousing/signature, international/footer and employment/guides. They could not be filtered by tag because the tags were the thing under test, so they were bracketed by timestamp and purged; geo-stats is back from 70 to 65. The remaining 2, employment and international at 04:11:46 and 04:11:50 with src=guides, sit inside the same iPhone session that was clicking the campaign links a minute either side, so **NO OUTSIDE PARTY DOWNLOADED A GUIDE TODAY.** User-agent capture on download rows shipped at 04:32, after all seven, so none of them carry a UA and the timing signature is the only evidence available; from tomorrow that separation is automatic. **SECOND SELF-INFLICTED POLLUTION OF THE DAY, SAME ROOT CAUSE**: verification traffic fired against production with genuine-looking tags. Both were caught and purged by exact timestamp brackets, and both would have been avoided by testing against a local server instead of the live site. That is now the rule for anything that writes.
- 2026-08-11 (pilot-status.html audited panel by panel; RR-127 completed and the auto-population handled it with no code change): Owner asked whether the links on pilot-status.html are updating. **AUDITED ALL ELEVEN DATA SOURCES THE PAGE ACTUALLY REQUESTS, NOT THE EIGHT THAT ARE OBVIOUS**: eight /api/*-stats endpoints plus three direct anon reads of pilot_progress, realcase_progress and armb_progress. Every one returns HTTP 200 with valid JSON. Rendered the page headless against real production payloads and asserted the end state rather than eyeballing it: zero panels left on "Loading", zero JS errors from the page, and every one of the 44 stat tiles populated. The only tile showing a placeholder is op-flagged, which is correct: flagged_condition_pct is null because no org-pilot records exist. Ten values were cross-checked tile against endpoint and all ten matched, so the page is rendering current data and not a cached copy. **A CACHING DEFECT WAS REPORTED AND THEN DISPROVED BY BETTER MEASUREMENT.** curl -L -I showed contributor-stats and orgpilot-stats with no Cache-Control, which would have meant those two panels could serve stale. Reading the source showed both set no-store, and re-checking against www without following the redirect confirmed it: the missing header belonged to the 307, not the endpoint. Seven of nine send no-store, asset-stats sends public/max-age=0/must-revalidate which revalidates every request, and panel-stats sends max-age=60 deliberately. No staleness anywhere. **THE HARNESS BUG FROM THE PANEL-STATS TURN REPEATED AND WAS CAUGHT THE SAME WAY**: the first audit run reported that the page requested only three sources and that every API-fed tile read zero, which would have been a serious finding. Playwright matches the last-registered route first and my catch-all was registered after the specific stubs, so it swallowed all eight API calls and returned {}. Re-ordered, all eleven appeared. Two ERR_CONNECTION_RESET entries are the Google Fonts requests, which cannot resolve in this sandbox, and are not a page defect. **RR-127 CANDID OPRIS COMPLETED TODAY AT 18:23Z, ALL 24 READS IN ONE SITTING, VERIFIED WITH check_completion.py, EXIT 0.** RR-117 also shows 24 today. Arm A had no activity. **THIS IS THE FIRST PROOF THE AUTO-POPULATION WORKS IN PRODUCTION**: /api/panel-stats already returned 56 reviewers and 35 completers with no code change and no intervention, and the six public pages were rendering those live values while their markup fallbacks still read 55 and 34. That is exactly the failure the endpoint was built to prevent after the same drift went unnoticed for most of a day on 2026-08-11. RR-127 had zero reads before today, so the graded figure moves as well as the completed one. **THE WARNING BRANCH ADDED TO build_expert_roster.py THIS MORNING FIRED CORRECTLY AND UNPROMPTED**: 35 live completers against 34 transcribed entries, naming the drift instead of silently reporting the roster length as it used to. Added RR-127 (Founder and Managing Partner, Opris & Associates; AI and data governance and digital trust; Canada, Greater Toronto Area; B2). **COUNTRIES STAY AT 16 AND THIS WAS RECOMPUTED, NOT ASSUMED**: Candid Opris is Canada, which rises from 3 to 4 completers but was already represented. Continents stay 5. Published figures are now 56 reviewers, 35 completers, 16 countries, 5 continents, and the markup fallbacks on all six pages were refreshed so a fetch failure cannot fall back to a stale number. **RECOGNITION FOR RR-127 IS NOT ISSUED AND IS OWNER-SIDE**: the completion is verified, but the certificate, message and recommendation were not requested and the Arm B blind still holds while RR-108 is unfinished.
- 2026-08-11 (the evaluation funnel zeros are real, and the phone screenshot was showing a page-level horizontal scroll): Owner sent a phone screenshot of the Reviewer Evaluation Funnel reading zero across all five tiles and asked why it is blank. **TWO SEPARATE THINGS, ONE REAL DEFECT AND ONE CORRECT NUMBER.** The defect is visual and had nothing to do with the funnel: the screenshot shows every heading clipped at the left edge, which is the whole page scrolled sideways. Measured at 390x844 with mobile emulation, documentElement.scrollWidth was 400 against a 390 viewport. Cause is the three data tables, whose columns cannot fit a phone. Wrapped each in a .tablewrap container with overflow-x auto so the TABLE scrolls and the DOCUMENT does not; scrollWidth is now 390 and the page-level overflow is gone. The tables still measure 400 internally, which is correct, they now scroll within their own box. **THE ZEROS ARE A TRUE ZERO AND THAT WAS PROVED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, BECAUSE THIS MORNING A COUNTER READING ZERO TURNED OUT TO BE BROKEN.** Fired a real GET at the live instrument with an unsuppressed e2echeck tag: the row was written to interaction_events as source eval-view carrying src, country, user agent and device. **MY FIRST READING OF THAT TEST WAS WRONG AND THE ENDPOINT'S OWN ACCOUNTING FIELD CORRECTED IT.** asset-stats still reported opened 0, which I initially recorded as the counter being broken. It was not: the request came from curl/8.5.0, and the crawler filter added earlier today matches curl, so the row was written and then correctly excluded. opened_crawlers_excluded read 1, which is exactly the field added so an exclusion could never be silent, and it is the reason the wrong conclusion survived less than a minute. Purged the check row; opened and opened_crawlers_excluded are both back to 0. **CONCLUSION: THE WRITE PATH WORKS, THE FILTER WORKS, AND NOBODY HAS OPENED THE EVALUATION.** The panel is not blank and not broken. The note already on the page states it accurately: the instrument opened on 9 August and the open counter on 10 August, so a zero is the expected state rather than a measured result. It has never been sent to anyone, which remains the single highest-value owner action.
